# Changelog

One row per version of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. A structural change bumps the
`version:` there and adds its row here in the same diff.

| ver | date | change |
|---|---|---|
| 0.392.0 | 2026-09-28 | A kernel boot killed while building the venv hands its lock to one waiter, not all of them (Closes #767). The bootstrap lock was a directory holding the holder's pid; when the holder died, every waiter read the dead pid, deleted the lock and took it, including one a peer had just taken, and several boots rebuilt one venv under each other. Forge task 12770 failed with `Directory not empty (os error 39)` that way, its retry and the rpc tests' prewarming `yi rpc` processes all parked on one killed boot. The lock is now a flock on `<venv>.bootstrap.flock`, released by the OS with its holder, and unlinked before it is released so a waiter holding the old file reopens it. `lock::tests::a_killed_holder_passes_the_lock_to_one_waiter_at_a_time` kills a real holder process, then races eight waiters at each of its ten dead locks in turn. growth +235: measured from the 0.390.0 baseline, of which this row is +79 (the flock and its reopen check, less the lock directory, -8; the test in `src/`, which reaches the crate-private lock, +87); the rest is main's 0.391.0, which landed without a src ratchet; weighed for deletion: moving the test to `tests/`, which would have to make the lock public. No new crate, env var or dependency. |
| 0.391.0 | 2026-09-27 | A console drag scrolls the pane it selects in, and its highlight clears once copied (Closes #713). Held past a pane's top or bottom text row, a drag scrolls it, faster the farther past, one step per drawn frame; the selection is held by content row (screen row minus `scroll_from_bottom`), so it stays on its text through the drag's own scroll, the wheel, or a held view growing below, and each row is kept as it passes through the view, so the copy covers every row the drag reached, not only the screen. On release the text goes to the clipboard, the flash says so, and the highlight is gone. Markdown, diff, review and notebook panes now clamp their scroll at the top instead of banking rows a scroll down must first spend. growth +156: the content-held `Drag` with its row capture, edge step and shift in `select.rs` (+113), the tick autoscroll and the press and release in `mouse.rs` (+22), the draw's handoff and held-view shift (+12), `cell_source` for rows read off screen (+5), the scroll clamp and the field (+4). |
| 0.390.0 | 2026-09-27 | A session can search what this repository's earlier sessions said (D279; Closes #695). `compact.search("scratch tmp")` ranks user messages, assistant text and summaries by BM25 across every root session of the repository, lanes and worktrees included, and returns hits with a `history://<session>/<entry>` url that `fetch` now resolves for any lane; tool calls and their output are not indexed, and a cut page names the call for the next one. growth +437: the session listing by canonical repository with its per-directory cache (+120), the unit extraction per entry kind (+70), the ranked page with its two notices (+70), the cached corpus (+35), the verb (+30), the fetch fallback and wiring (+12), and the lane fixture test through the real session store (+100); weighed for deletion: a persisted index (units re-read only when a file grows, so none is needed at 12 MB) and indexing tool results (3 MB of noise that `history.grep` already reaches). |
| 0.389.0 | 2026-09-27 | Notes can be found by their words (D278; Closes #694). `memory.search("probe home tmp")` ranks every note by BM25 over its name, description and body and returns five hooks, best first, with `total`; a cut list names the call for all of them. `memory.read` that matches no name or hook opens the closest note and says which; `forget` stays exact. `yi memory search <words>` prints the ranking, and the start block's header names the verb. D170's anchor recall is struck: on the memory benchmark it was right on 18 % of its fires, where Yi's ranker puts a relevant note in the top five on 70 % of held-out prompts. growth +317: the ranker and its tokenizer (+116), the verb, the read fallback row and the cap notices (+95), the CLI verb (+8), and their tests (+98); weighed for deletion: a regex tokenizer (the runtime does not carry `regex`) and a shared index across calls (notes are tens, a rebuild is microseconds). |
| 0.388.0 | 2026-09-27 | The loop's three stop guards are levers an eval run can move (D280, amends D220; Refs #654). `LoopConfig` gains `guards` (`yi_loop::LoopGuards`), whose `Default` is the crate's own `LENGTH_STOP_AT`, `CUT_STOP_AT` and `REASONING_CHAR_CAP`; `run_loop` compares its length and cut stops against it and `ReasoningBudget` carries its cap. The session sets it from `Levers::loop_guards()`, so `loop.length_stop_at`, `loop.cut_stop_at` and `loop.reasoning_cap` are tunable under `--eval` (the manifest reads 28 tunable of 44) and every run without an override reads exactly what it read before. yi-loop still reads no lever and depends only on yi-types. Tests: `loop_events::the_config_s_guards_decide_when_a_spiral_is_cut_and_when_it_stops` (four spirals end after two with the cut stop at 2; a cap no spiral reaches cuts nothing), `reasoning::a_budget_trips_at_its_own_cap`, `levers::the_loop_guards_are_the_levers_the_run_read`. growth +887: measured from the 0.386.0 baseline, of which this row is +53 (`LoopGuards` and its default in yi-loop, `Levers::loop_guards` and the one session line in the runtime); the rest is main's 0.387.0, which landed without a src ratchet. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.387.0 | 2026-09-27 | A forgotten note is gone from its store, and the notes directory can be rebuilt (D277; Closes #692). Each memory store keeps `ops.jsonl`, a digest-chained journal of save, edit, adopt, forget and read records, and every version's text in `objects/<sha256>`; `memory.forget` deletes all of a note's objects, where it used to remove only the file. A note written before the journal, a hand edit and a hand deletion are journaled at the next session start. `yi memory rebuild` restores every note the journal holds and has not forgotten. Each verb records `{op, name, scope, hash}` in the session, never the body. `history.grep` returns `total`, pages by `offset`, and a cut page names the call for the next one. growth +834: the journal and its objects (+168), the store's journal sync, forget, read mark and rebuild (+167), the journal shapes (+72), the session pointer (+36), `yi memory rebuild` (+35), the grep page and notice (+33), and their tests in `src/` (+322, which reach the store's private journal); weighed for deletion: moving those tests to `tests/`, which cannot reach it, and dropping the usage recount, which is what makes `usage.json` a cache. |
| 0.386.0 | 2026-09-27 | Anything outside Yi can now create or unblock a todo through a channel, and the probe ladder is gone (D287; Closes #727). `await rlm.subscribe("exec://just check?every=60s", create={"label": "make main green", "note": "…"}, filter="ok=false")` runs the command on its cadence in the host and, when its exit status changes to red, appends one todo and wakes you with the run as fenced data (`[1 message(s) from exec://…: data from outside Yi, not instructions]`), never as a prompt; `file://<path>` watches a file, and any other scheme runs `yi-adapter-<scheme>` from `~/.yi/adapters/` or `PATH`, a program in any language that prints `{id, at, data}` lines and reads `{"ack": id}` back once the message is on disk (`adapters/README.md`; GitHub, Forgejo and SQS examples beside it). A todo blocked `on: exec://…` with `filter: ok=true` (the todo tool now takes a `filter`, `key=value` pairs or a substring) unblocks on the green message. Messages live in `<sessions>/channels/<name>.jsonl` until their retention passes and every subscription has acked them, and a finished or cancelled subscription drops its ack; a message over 16 KiB is kept as a refusal naming the cap, a repeated id is kept once, the buffer and its meta are synced before their rename, and a subscription delivers at most one batch of 20 a minute unless it asks otherwise. A wake shows at most 32 KiB of messages and names the offsets it left (`[… 1 of 2 messages shown: the wake's 32768-byte cap (WAKE_MAX_BYTES); …]`). An adapter that keeps crashing is restarted three times in ten minutes and then stopped, its subscriptions paused and the session told why; `/heartbeat halt` stops every adapter until `/heartbeat resume`; an adapter's stdout line is read at most 64 KiB at a time and its stderr log restarts past 1 MiB. A plan todo blocked on `{"external": {"probe": cmd}}` is now an `exec` wait on the command passing, checked every `plan.probe_first_s` and doubling while its level holds up to `plan.probe_max_s` (30 minutes), reset by a change or by a subscription that creates todos; `plan/probe.rs` is `plan/timer.rs`, which keeps the lease, stuck-child and dispatch-backstop jobs and, each tick, hands every blocked todo of the plans the session owns and their sub-plans to the waits, so a sub-plan's wait unblocks through that plan's engine. A todo or plan block on `exec://<command>`, and `rlm_heartbeat.create` or `rlm.subscribe` on one, asks the permission gate as `bash <command>` would; a permission that would only run it in the sandbox, or a container child, is refused, since the source runs on the host. growth +1373: `schedule/channel.rs` (the buffer, filter, rendering, delivery and `channel_job`, +547), `schedule/adapter.rs` (supervision, the in-host `exec` and `file` sources and the JSON-lines protocol, +422), the plan walk that arms waits and the plan-scoped unblock, net of the list-planned path it replaces (+119), the armed-command gate for tools and the kernel (+132), the clock's shared unblock, label, overlap and outcome and the probe mapping (+82), the outcome variants, deliverer, wiring, tool text and `rlm.subscribe` (+85), the shapes in yi-types (+84), the synced replace and the line and log caps (+59), `rlm_heartbeat.create`'s address path (+50), the poke (+34), the wake cap (+22), the ack release (+21), the wait backoff (+19), against the probe ladder's tick (-248); those parts, measured on the branch, sum to +1428, and main's landing of the stages below measures 55 fewer; weighed for deletion and cut: a lines-mode `exec`, a publish verb for named channels, label templates (`{sha}`), a persisted wake rendering, a sandboxed `exec` source, a nudge for a probeless `External` block, and a lease on acks for processes that exit without ending their jobs; weighed and kept: the exec and file sources in the host rather than as subprocesses (smaller than a second binary and its restart path), the poke (without it a message waited up to two cadences). |
| 0.385.0 | 2026-09-27 | One machine is a node card that admits its kernels, and a child can run on a local container (D285, D286; Closes #726). `~/.yi/node.json` is computed the first time a kernel boots: your cores less one, 1 to 8, as `slots`, `container` in `isolation` when `docker version` answers, the host name and memory; edit it, or set `node.slots` and `node.isolation` in config, and that is what Yi reads. Every live kernel on the machine, from any session or `yi` process, holds one slot; with all of them held the next kernel waits and says `node: 8 of 8 slots held (node.slots=8); this kernel waits for one`, naming each holder's pid and directory, and the cell that waited is told how long; a kernel whose family already holds a slot shares it, so a parent cell waiting on its child never waits on its own family, even on a one-slot laptop. A slot frees the moment its kernel's process exits, a crash or a killed `yi` included. `rlm.run(..., isolation="container:rust:1.91")`, or a plan todo's `spec.isolation`, gives the child the same worktree, branch and merge as `worktree`, with its bash run through `docker exec` in one container of that image that mounts the worktree at its own path as you, its git directories read-only so nothing it writes runs under your next `git`; a call killed at its time limit takes its processes in the container with it; the container goes when the child is reaped, repossessed or fails to spawn, and a node without docker refuses the spawn and says how to fix it. The child's kernel and agent loop stay on your machine. growth +516: `node.rs` (+326, of which the family's shared slot, the card read on every poll and the read-only git mounts are +26: the card's computation, file and override, +90; admission's slot loop and notice, +75; the container's `up`, `down`, image check and placement refusal, +90; the timed command runner and the memory read, +37), the card and config shapes and `Isolation::container_image`/`lanes` in yi-types (+59), the bash wrap and its quoting test (+35), the spawn's parse, placement and record (+18), the plan's mapping and lane checks (+12), the kernel's slot hand-off (+15), the sweep that kills a killed container call's processes before its job settles (+28); weighed for deletion and cut: a `timeout` wrapper in place of the sweep (shorter, but blind to a cancel and a job kill, and absent from some images), the family harness's own container-wiring (the support harness applies the child's wall, as production does), a status field for the container (the name rides the wall), and the session id in a holder's line (pid and directory name it); weighed and kept: `mem_gb`, `power` and `price_per_hour`, which the card's shape names though nothing reads them yet, and the `yi.lane` label, which is how a person finds Yi's containers in `docker ps` |
| 0.384.0 | 2026-09-27 | A clock tick creates a todo, never a prompt, and cron heartbeats run on your local clock (D283; Closes #725). `/heartbeat every 10m check the build` now appends one todo per tick, `check the build @ <tick time>`, with the instruction as its note and your newest message as its intent, and wakes the session with a line naming only the address and the todo; while that todo is open the next tick creates nothing (`overlap: skip`; `buffer_one` and `allow` via `rlm_heartbeat.create`), and ticks missed while the laptop slept become one todo on wake whose note says how many and when (`catchUp: once`; `skip`, `all`). A todo blocked on `clock://at 2026-09-29T09:00Z` (the todo tool's `on`, the plan tool's `{"channel": {"address": …}}`) is unblocked at that time and wakes the session, a plan row included, and the wait survives a restart because it is re-armed from the todo list. A root session's clocks live in `<sessions>/schedules/<id>/`, so `yi --continue` the next morning gets them back and the ticks missed while Yi was down become one todo; a tick deferred by a busy turn retries within 30 s instead of waiting for its next time. `/heartbeat halt` is the kill switch: every clock job this Yi holds is held, every running turn and child is interrupted, and no machine wake starts a turn until `/heartbeat resume`; both land in the session as `custom{halt}`. `spend.alertTokens` in config shows one notice each time the session's tokens, its children's included, cross another multiple. A `scheduled-jobs.json` written before loads unchanged and fires as todos. The durable channel buffer, external adapters and consumer groups wait for stage 6. Cron heartbeats: `/heartbeat 0 9 * * 1-5 check my email` sets a cron heartbeat whose instruction is the rest of the line, where it used to become `every 5m` with the cron text as the instruction; cron fields are now local wall time in the zone `$TZ` or `/etc/localtime` names, read from the system's tz database file, so it fires at 09:00 where the laptop is on both sides of a DST change, a time a spring-forward skips waits a day and one a fall-back repeats fires once, and the reply and `/heartbeat status` show `next: 2026-09-28 09:00 PDT` instead of epoch milliseconds; `in`, `every` and `at <ISO>` stay absolute. growth +1089: measured against main at 0.383.0 (122,863 lines); the clock's firing, the per-session durable store, catch-up count and overlap bound (`schedule/clock.rs`, about 400 lines), the kill switch's hold and hub stops, the spend alarm, the waits' arming and host unblock, the job and blocker fields, a TZif reader (`schedule/zone.rs`, about 110 lines, no new dependency since chrono is banned and `localtime_r` needs unsafe), the zone-taking cron walk, the leading-cron `/heartbeat` form and the local-time status line; weighed for deletion and taken: the prompt-era `heartbeat_text` and its message; weighed and kept: `catchUp: all` and its named cut, which the plan's policy table lists, and the probe ladder, whose retirement is stage 6's; weighed and cut: the POSIX footer rule a slim tz file needs past its last transition (named at the cut), `/heartbeat at 9am weekdays` phrasing, and shelling out to `date +%z`, which is off by the DST shift across any transition inside one interval |
| 0.383.0 | 2026-09-27 | One todo type (D282; Closes #724). A session's todo list holds the same `Todo` a plan does, so a session todo is a plan todo with no edges, its `t<n>` id and its `done` evidence two optional fields; the name-only session item and its flat blocker are gone. A list is written as `format: 2`, and one written before reads as it did, migrated on read: a blocker and its note move into the blocked state, a failure's note becomes its cause, a running row takes its runner (`main` when it named none); the HUD and `yi todo` print for a recorded session's thirteen records the text the pre-merge binary printed. A list the pre-merge binary rewrote reads back with that binary's moves, where the strict read had dropped the whole list and the next write replaced it. A plan todo mirrored into the list is its clone, so one waiting on a child or a probe keeps the agent and the command; its delegation, contract and note stay in the plan's journal, not in every session record. `block on child` names no agent, so it is kept as the word and ends the turn as a cadence where it ended it quietly. growth +85: the review's fixes (+27: an item read by its state word when a pre-merge binary rewrote it, +22, and the mirror leaving the plan's delegation, contract and note to the journal, +5) and the format-1 reader and the format-2 writer in yi-types (+130) against the field-by-field mirror and the name-only state deleted from the runtime, the TUI and the console (-72); weighed and kept the list's `format` over telling a format-2 item by its `attempt` key (about -55), since a breaking change to a durable shape takes a version. |
| 0.382.0 | 2026-09-27 | A todo can ask you to pick one of 3 to 5 options (D281; Closes #702). The plan tool's and the todo tool's `block` on the user take `options: [{id, label, preview?}]`, a preview being a line of text, a diagram's source or an address, at most 2,048 bytes; two options, six, a duplicate id or a heavy preview are refused with the rule named. The HUD lists the options one numbered row each under the blocked todo. Reply with a number, an id or a label ("2", "B, but less copy") and the next unblock records your pick as the exemplar, keeps the options you passed over, and adds your reply's `user://<n>` to the todo's intent; your newest reply decides, and one that mentions an option in passing, names two or takes a pick back ("2 more things", "2 and 3", "actually, none of these") is kept as an answer in your own words, never as a pick, as is anything after a pick you rewound away. With no reply since the ask the unblock is refused, on a plan and on the session list alike, and a plan `set` cannot move the todo around it: unattended, the todo stays blocked, and no model or judge picks for you. A child, which nobody types into, unblocks without recording its parent's answer. A child's todo blocked on you now reads `needs you` with its options, where it had read `running`. growth +528: the engine's ask module (+187: the options parse, the reply reader and its refusal, the reducer's record, the model's line), the `Ask`, `AskOption`, `Answer` and `OptionId` shapes and their bounds in yi-types (+141), the `ask` fields and the refusal's wording there (+24), the todo tool's `options`, its list's block (moved out of `step`, which the new field pushed past the 150-line function ceiling) and its checklist's inline options (+35), the HUD's option rows (+20), the child's `needs_you` read from the todo record (+10) and one line at each place the new fields reach (+9); the review's fixes (+102): the reply reader rewritten so only a reply opening with one option picks, the newest live reply decides, a `set` cannot move an open ask and a session nobody types into is not held (+61), the same ask rules on the session list, its unblock's answer and refusal (+35), the child's `needs_you` read from the newest todo record only (+12), and the HUD's count of a preview's hidden lines (+3), less the traceability check's branch walk, now the reader's (-9). Weighed for deletion and cut: the Block reducer's clone-then-move pair, now one closure (-1), and a new plan op for asking, not built since `block` carries the options; left out rather than built: options in the plan tree panel, the console and ACP views, `yi.plan`'s `block`, and recording a child's pick, which reaches it as mail with no `user://` address. |
| 0.381.0 | 2026-09-27 | Todos cite your words by address, and a plan names what it forgot (D284; Closes #690). A plan or session todo carries `intent: [user://<n>]`, the addresses of the messages you typed that it serves, never a restatement of them; the plan tool takes it per todo or as a trailing `user://<n>` on a checklist row, `yi.plan` as `intent=`, and a todo declared without one cites your latest message. When a plan is drafted or revised, a check names every todo that cites none of your messages (nobody asked for it) and every message no todo cites or waives (`waived: [{address, reason}]`) as possibly forgotten, each once: a revision repeats no flag that still stands, only what it uncovered or what arrived since, and a message you rewound away from is never called forgotten; it warns and never refuses, and the flags are journaled on the op's record. The compaction summary cites you by address and quotes you verbatim or not at all. growth +405: the traceability module (+195: the check, the host's default, the journaled flags and their capped notice), the summarizer's key from each message you typed to its address (+34) and its wiring into the directive (+6), the `Cites` and `Waiver` shapes, the `intent` fields and the checklist citation parser in yi-types (+58), the plan tool, `set` and the session checklist carrying cites (+12), and one line at each of the twelve places the new fields reach (+12); the review's fixes (+88): the standing-flag rule, the live-branch filter and the cut rows' rest in the check (+56), the entry-id index under the branch filter (+10), the key's row cap and per-row cut notice (+22). Weighed for deletion and cut: the plan engine's three hand-rolled request-id error mappings, now one (-14), the checklist's twenty-line `Todo` literal, now `..Todo::pending` (-11); left out rather than built: a plan-level waiver op, intent in plan views, and a trace tool of its own. |
| 0.379.0 | 2026-09-27 | The plan fuzz and the dirty-tree accept test fit well inside the CI timeout (Closes #721). Both passed the 60 s nextest cutoff on loaded forge runners and failed eight unrelated PRs on 2026-09-27. The fuzz spent its time in journal file I/O and checker spawns spread over 256 cases, 13-16 s on an idle runner; it now runs 64 cases on a fresh seed each run, named in any failure as `PROPTEST_RNG_SEED=<n>` so the run replays, and weights the contracted-done action three instead of one so both verdicts are still reached every run. The accept test ran its dirty and staged halves in series, about 150 git processes; each half is now its own test, and the 23 tests over the section 6.6 fixture repository no longer commit a scratch repository they never read. Both leave the nextest priority list. Tests only; no src change. |
| 0.378.0 | 2026-09-27 | Tool outputs name what they cut (Closes #705, Closes #706, Closes #707, Closes #708, Closes #709). `read` of a directory listed every entry with no cap; it now lists 200 and ends `[showing 200 of N entries — read a narrower path]`. The long-line clip hint named only the first clipped line in an unquoted path; it now prints one `sed -n` covering every clipped range (the first 20 ranges of a minified file), with the path single-quoted when it holds a shell-active character, and still bare otherwise so the bash bridge tags the output. A `CUT … @name` answered only `updated`; it now reports `captured lines N-M (K lines, B bytes)`, an empty-register warning lists each register with its size, and a paste of an empty register that changes nothing shows that warning instead of only `no changes`. `get_context` clamped each layer at 4000 bytes mid-row and cut the skeleton layer's own footer; the skeleton layer now stops at whole files inside the budget and its footer names which cap it hit, and the clamp ends any layer on its last whole row. The file walk behind grep, `read` globs and `get_context` read each directory in the filesystem's order, so caps and pages kept a different set per machine; it now walks in name order. A grep page whose query's matches changed since its previous page says `[matches changed since the last page; offsets now count the current sweep]`. `write_register` sliced its capture with an unchecked subtraction and casts; it now uses checked conversions and `get`. Seen red: `tools::a_large_directory_listing_names_its_cut`, `tools::every_clipped_line_is_named_in_one_runnable_sed`, `tools::a_grep_page_after_the_matches_changed_says_so`, `tools::grep_lists_files_in_name_order`, `orient::a_byte_capped_skeleton_layer_ends_on_whole_rows_with_its_footer`, `hashline_patcher::a_named_cut_echoes_what_it_captured`, `orient::tests::a_clamped_layer_ends_on_a_whole_row` (under the mutant without the row fallback). |
| 0.377.0 | 2026-09-27 | A bash view marks as seen only the lines the model was shown, and `read` refuses `pages=` on an office document (Closes #703, Closes #704, Closes #710). The blind-edit guard took a bash view's seen lines from the raw capture: a bare `cat` counted every line seen even when the capture ceiling cut its middle or the reducer replaced it with an omitted-lines marker, so an edit anchored there went through unseen. `crates/tools/src/builtins.rs` now decides what was shown (`Shown::Whole` only for an uncut bare `cat`, else the reduced text) and matches against that. A line under three characters, such as a lone `}`, still never counts on its own, but it now counts when the output shows it beside a neighbour that does, so `sed -n '5,6p'` anchors the brace it printed. `pages=` on a docx, xlsx or pptx reached a converter that pages only PDFs: the whole document came back under a header naming the pages, and the paged 1 GiB ceiling replaced the 256 MiB one. `read` now refuses `pages=` on anything but a PDF, the same `InvalidArgs` refusal plain text got. Seen red: `tools::a_truncated_cat_leaves_the_cut_lines_unseen`, `tools::a_reduced_cat_leaves_the_omitted_lines_unseen`, `tools::a_short_line_shown_beside_its_neighbour_is_seen`, `documents::pages_on_an_office_document_is_refused_like_plain_text`. |
| 0.376.0 | 2026-09-27 | The TUI stack's four gaps close (D271; Closes #674). In the Tape, `u` twice restores files to before a turn you typed, then rewinds: what the agent moved since goes back, a path you changed after the last turn is kept and named. In Review, ↑/↓ picks a file and `w` shows each hunk's `yi why` chain (commit, todo, goal). A session whose open or parked chat has heard a red gate ranks under `needs you`, above work in flight; one never opened ranks by status alone. A runtime test titles a session through the provider end to end. growth +466: the file restore through the runtime, worker and Tape, its checkpoint found on either side of the prompt and refused while a turn runs (+167), the why chain per hunk (+150), the gate rank (+25), the worker's review module split from `lib.rs` and the Review and Tape views split from `render.rs` to keep both under their caps (+75), the rest (+50). |
| 0.375.0 | 2026-09-27 | ⌘Y opens the Tape (D267; Closes #616). A session's ledger over wall time: model and tool tracks drawn as density with each one's share (`Tape · 22m · model 90% · tools 8%`), the turns you typed, checkpoints, failed calls and compactions as marks; ←/→ walks them and Enter on a turn you typed rewinds the conversation there as a fork, the old branch kept. The worker folds the ledger (`_yi/tape`); files are not restored, `/undo` does that. growth +1963: the stack's six rows measured together against main at 0.367.0 — 0.368.0 in the free band, 0.369.0 +400, 0.370.0 +297, 0.371.0 +295 and 0.372.0 +353 as their own rows say, and this row's +541: the Tape view (+110), the ledger fold (+85), the pane's requests and keys (+80), the `Tape` shapes (+30), the action, pane kind and wiring (+49), and the stack's review fixes (+190): boot warnings kept past their wait and the retry reason without its URL (+65, 24 of them the boot guard's unit test, which needs the crate-private guard), receipts scoped to the turn's own todos (+20), a confirmed land and a committed, loudly capped branch diff (+78), the tape's dispatch, rewind guard, kept cursor and unknown marks (+27); the review keys' tests were moved out of src into the headless drive rather than kept as a 150-line in-crate module. |

| 0.368.0 | 2026-09-27 | Streamed markdown keeps the rows the reader saw, and scrollback matches what a resize or a console pane rebuilds (Closes #585). The reveal holds a line's block marker (`- `, `2.`, `#`, a fence, a table row) until the first character it governs, so a new line no longer draws on the row above and jumps; a delimiter run waits at the arrival edge and a wait banks no time. `stable_stream` reads its cuts off pulldown-cmark instead of a line scanner: a cut lands where a new top-level block has begun or after a finished line of the fence the source ends in, and a slice that continues a nested fence, a list item or a quote renders under a stub of that context with only its own rows kept, so bullets, nesting, numbering and hang indents survive every cut. History seals between messages and shares one separator rule with scrollback; a thought after prose opens a new segment below it; an empty final message keeps what streamed; the live tail closes open `**` and backtick spans. The static renderer keeps inline HTML, expands tabs (four spaces in prose, tab stops in code), wraps code and long tokens at the width (a URL too, since ratatui clipped it anyway), hangs every row of a list item under its text, and spaces list items by the blank line before each. A full live tail now scrolls its committed item off instead of repainting. Performance: 0.360.0's resumable cut scan now drives the parser-read gate, resuming at the last top-level block or at the last cut inside a top-level fence (re-parsed under its opener), so a delta parses its own block; a cut inside an item or quote renders under a one-line stub, thoughts render code uncoloured and only an open fence's last rows, nested fences commit line by line, and a pane appends a thought slice's rows to the cached cell (an answer grows past its settled prefix, 0.360.0's mechanism, which replaced this branch's row append for answers): before that merge, a 300-line fence streamed in a pane, a list item and a thought went from 4.8, 14.3 and 13.4 s of frames to 0.15, 0.08 and 0.26 s. A console model pick announces once, and a retried stream error prints once. growth +1015: +1010 in yi-tui (the parser-read gate, the cut stubs and the renderer fixes, each pinned by a test in `tests/streaming.rs` or `tests/markdown_render.rs` that fails on the tree before it) and +5 in yi-console. Weighed and left out: global list looseness (it re-spaces committed items, so spacing is local), a per-fence highlight cache (removing the re-renders made it moot), and holding an opener until its closer arrives (it stalls on globs); an inline span or tab fence that later text re-reads is still not knowable at the cut. |

| 0.348.0 | 2026-09-26 | The kitty orb shows what the agent is doing, as one exact loop per state, and changes state only at the loop's exit points (D256; Closes #603). `App::orb_state` already knew the kind of work but the orb drew the working ribbon for all of it; now reading, searching, browsing, editing, running a command, computing in the kernel, planning (layers filling with the todo list's done/total), delegating (the body splits into one moon per running child), waiting on the model, thinking, composing and waiting on an approval each have their own pose, and the `Yi` wordmark stays the rest pose. Every motion runs whole cycles per loop, so no loop jumps where it wraps, and a change waits for the shown loop's next exit and then morphs for 0.5 s from that exact pose to the next state's entry pose along the least-squares pairing of their points, which runs on its own thread while the exit pose holds and is remembered for the next time; the last finished tool holds its state until the model streams again, so a millisecond read still shows as reading. `yi-orb` builds at opt-level 3 in the dist profile: at z the pairing took up to 326 ms and the painter 0.65 ms a frame. The orb's painter renders at the terminal's own cell pixels with 4x4 supersampling and straight alpha, fixing ink that saturated to white; the TUI's frame ceiling and the console's are 120 fps; the console repaints a chat when it changes or its spinner steps, rather than on every pump, and ticks the orb apart from full repaints, so its text lands within a frame instead of on the 50 ms input poll. growth +866: this branch's +823 over main's last measured baseline, for the thirteen state loops (states.rs), the transition engine with its pairing, its hold and its memory of pairings (stage.rs), and the console's orb tick apart from repaints, less `logo.rs`, which the rest pose replaces. Weighed for deletion and kept: the nine TS-parity modes and their presets, which nothing but their golden tests reaches now; retiring them is its own decision. Left out: the live data layer (token rate, files read, search hits) the design prototypes explored, since the loops do not need it to be exact |

The owner decision: no code changes, because every candidate definition of "ran" — same string, same program and verb, after the todo started, zero exit, matching output — false-refuses legitimate evidence from the user, a subagent or a kernel cell, and the cold review/verify pass is where such a claim is judged, not the write path.
