| 0.339.0 | 2026-09-26 | The public mirror covers the fixture OAuth token literal (Closes #548). Commit 59ea8762 added `crates/types/tests/fixtures/oauth-token-v1.json` with key-shaped fixture values (`fixture-oauth-token` and its `-refresh` sibling); a later commit replaced the tree's current values, but the mirror publishes unrewritten history, so the old blob still carried the literal and `scripts/mirror/sync.sh --check` failed `public_surface --stdin`. `scripts/mirror/replace.txt` gains one literal rule for `fixture-oauth-token`, covering both values (the refresh one as a substring). |
| 0.338.0 | 2026-09-26 | `/undo` moves only the paths the turn changed (D248; Closes #531). The restore scope is the diff between the turn's start and end trees (`--no-renames`, so a renamed file comes back instead of failing the undo); a path the turn wrote that the user edited afterwards is kept and named in a `[kept N of M — changed since the turn ended, left as you have it: …]` row on the CLI, ACP and TUI; a redo moves only what the undo moved, through the `after` tree the `Undo` entry now records; a target with no turn end to pair with restores unscoped as before and says so in an `[unscoped …]` row. Before, `yi undo` reverted every hand edit and deleted every hand-created file since the turn started. The TUI refuses `/undo` while the turn runs, as ACP does. |
| 0.337.0 | 2026-09-26 | Plans keep recording into `.yi/plans/` under the process cwd, and `.yi/schemas/` is now generated and ignored (D254; Closes #572). `PlanStore::publish` (crates/runtime/src/plan/store.rs) writes a `.gitignore` of `*` into `.yi/schemas/` the first time it creates that directory — the same shape it already writes `*/ops.jsonl` into `.yi/plans/` — so the schema file it republishes from `PLAN_SCHEMA` on every open never shows as an untracked blob. `plan.json` is unaffected: it stays the git-tracked document D97/D193 made it. The cross-worktree case (a plan recorded in the lane rather than the tree the work is about) is left to #445 (movable workdir). |
| 0.336.0 | 2026-09-26 | A negative or non-numeric rlm timeout is refused naming the value, and a miss on a child raises one `rlm.NoSuchChild` (Closes #569). `timeout_of` mapped any `timeout_ms` that was not a whole non-negative number to the five-minute cap, so `rlm.wait(-5)` answered `timeout_ms: 300000, clamped: false` and `rlm.receive(-1)` slept the whole cap; it now returns `Result`, and `rlm.wait`, `rlm.receive` and `agent_message.request` refuse with the value, so `clamped` compares against what the caller sent. The kernel's `wait`, `receive` and `request` refuse the same seconds before sending. `key_of`'s miss names the children there are, as `rlm.status` does, and the kernel raises `NoSuchChild(RuntimeError, KeyError)` for it and for `status`, so `result`, `delete_subagent` and `status` fail one way and existing `except KeyError` and `except RuntimeError` both still catch it. |
| 0.335.0 | 2026-09-26 | The todo and plan tools refuse unknown and closed todos truthfully and print a stepped row once (Closes #570). A label on a plan-mirrored list is refused `Mirrored` only when the plan's rows hold it; a label that exists nowhere gets the not-found error that lists the known ids. `todo done` on a closed item refuses as `plan done` does (`done is not a move for "read" in state done`) instead of overwriting the evidence that closed it, and a phase or all target skips closed items, so a dropped row is not reopened as done. `plan start` no longer prints the running row twice: `body` skips the todo `render` led with. An over-long label in `plan init`/`append` names its `todos[i]` and the 80-char cap, which the `todos` schema now states (+19 bytes of priced prefix). A `todo set` that replaces a released plan's rows says `replaced plan <id>'s N rows; that plan is no longer open`. |
| 0.334.0 | 2026-09-26 | The hashline patcher and grep refuse the requests they used to guess at (D253; Closes #565, Closes #566, Closes #568). An empty `PUT N.=M:` / `PUT N*:` body, a body row without `+` (or a blank row inside a body) and `PUT >N*:` on a line that opens no block each return an error naming the right form (`CUT N.=M` / `CUT N*`, `+TEXT` / `+`, an opener line or plain `PUT >N:`) instead of being applied with a warning the model read after the write; `PUT >N*` on a closing delimiter still lowers to `PUT >N:` with its warning. The seen-lines guard now covers what a view really showed: an unknown- or stale-tag rejection records the anchored lines it displays as seen (it recorded none, leaving the next untagged edit unrestricted), a `read` that showed no rows (`limit: 0`, offset past EOF) no longer counts as a full view, and a grep `replace` with `apply` marks only the lines its diff shows; prompt.md's rule now reads "touch only lines some view of this file has displayed". grep answers a `path` that does not exist with an error naming it (it said `No matches found`), refuses a negative or non-integer `offset` (it paged from 0), and reports a `context` it could not honour as `[context ignored: asked N, not a non-negative integer; 0 used]` beside the existing clamp notice. |
| 0.333.0 | 2026-09-26 | The bash tool's description no longer claims the cwd persists between calls (Closes #567). Each `bash -c` is a fresh process started by `jobs::run_or_background` in the session's working directory, so a `cd` in one call never reaches the next; the description now says so. #445 (movable workdir) is the place to change that for every tool at once. |
| 0.332.0 | 2026-09-26 | The environment block's git clause counts untracked paths apart from modified ones (Closes #571). `environment::git_summary` split `git status --porcelain` lines into modified and untracked (a `??` line) instead of counting every line as one `dirty` total, so two new untracked directories no longer read as "2 modified"; the new `git_line(branch, modified, untracked)` names both counts, omits either that is zero, and keeps the pre-existing "0 modified" on a clean tree. |
| 0.331.0 | 2026-09-26 | `get_context` spends its skeleton layer on the files the task is about (Closes #586). `skeletons` sorted up to 2,000 walked names and took the first 40, so in a workspace the layer went to `crates/a…` and the `symbol` argument reached only the neighborhood layer. Files are now ranked by whether they define `symbol` (a declaration line naming it), then merely mention it, then by the git change heat the packet already counts (`change_heat`, shared with the heat layer, so git runs once), then by name. The layer's first line states the order with a count per tier (defining, mentioning, with change heat, then name), placed first because the 4,000-byte layer clamp cuts from the end; the 40-file cut notice now names its cap and the next call. A `path` argument scoping the layer to a subtree is not added. |
| 0.330.0 | 2026-09-26 | The advisor's cadence review path is deleted (D251, amends D56; Closes #576). `AdvisorConfig::cadence`, `should_review` and `AdvisorState::calls_since_review` are gone: `grid uses AdvisorConfig` and a repo-wide grep found no production path or test that ever set the field, so `observe`'s trigger check is now the `forced` flag alone (plan transitions and compaction). |
| 0.329.0 | 2026-09-26 | The levers inventory names constants a config path can actually reach (D247, amends D220; Refs #534). `advisor.cadence` is deleted — no config key or code path can set it, `AdvisorConfig::cadence` stays `None` from the one production constructor — and `family.depth` now points at `yi_types::config::DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH`, the constant `RlmConfig::depth`'s `unwrap_or` already reads for an unset `rlm.maxDepth`, moved out of `subagent::DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH`, which had no other reader. The manifest drops from 45 to 44 levers. |
| 0.328.0 | 2026-09-26 | A heartbeat due while the session compacts, or while work waits queued behind a running turn, waits for its next tick (D246; Closes #535). The scheduler's deliverer filled only `is_streaming`, so `compact_now`, which summarizes while the session is idle, let a due steer heartbeat start a run that the summarized history then overwrote. `AgentSession::heartbeat_deliverer` reads the status, then the steer and follow-up queues (pending work only behind a running turn: an idle queue holds inboxed notices and held follow-ups), then `Compactor::compacting`, a flag `maybe_compact` holds up through a drop guard from the start of the summarizer work to its last exit; `wire_schedule` and `schedule_e2e` both use it, so the e2e runs production's deliverer instead of a hand copy. `SessionActivity` loses `is_retrying`, `is_bash_running` and `unfinished_action_count`, which nothing in yi filled. |
| 0.327.0 | 2026-09-26 | `/heartbeat` is a slash verb (Refs #535). `runtime::slash::SESSION_VERBS` gained `heartbeat`, dispatching to the same `HeartbeatService::run` that RPC `heartbeat` and ACP `_yi/heartbeat` already called, so the grammar reaches the solo TUI, the console and ACP `_yi/slash` — previously it answered `unknown command: /heartbeat` on every surface but RPC and `_yi/heartbeat` directly. ACP `_yi/slash` special-cases `heartbeat` to route through the `_yi/heartbeat` handler so the client still gets the `_yi/heartbeat_changed` notification. Restores design §15.2; the H8 defer-fill half of #535 is a separate PR. |
| 0.326.0 | 2026-09-26 | A compaction whose entry fails to write is not applied (Closes #529). `Compactor::maybe_compact` returned the compacted history even when `append_compaction` failed, so the live model ran on a summary a resume never loads, which then paid for a second summarizer call; it now returns `Result`, advances a copy of the window and hands back the replacement only once the store took the entry. On a failure the history and the window stay as they were, `store_error` records the error, and a `[compaction not saved: <error>; history left uncompacted, /compact retries]` row reaches the model at its next message boundary; the run continues. Auto-compaction pauses until an explicit `/compact` succeeds, so a disk that stays broken does not buy a summarizer call at every boundary. The RPC `compact` reports `applied:false`. The child-usage record's append failure, dropped the same way, now lands in `store_error` too. |
| 0.325.0 | 2026-09-26 | An advisor Hold that names no target degrades to a Warn instead of holding every call (Closes #528). The `advise` tool leaves `target` optional, and a Hold without one became an empty-pattern permission hold that every call's subject contains, so each call asked for an hour, in yolo too. The sink now builds a `HoldPattern`, which refuses an empty or blank target, and falls back to the D28 Warn path the no-asker case already takes; §16's "a Hold is a 1 h permission hold on the target, or a Warn with no asker" now reads "...or no target". |
| 0.324.0 | 2026-09-26 | Five pieces of dead code and drift are deleted (Refs #534). `vendor/rtk/` (75 tracked files, ~15k lines) is removed: it was never a workspace member, its phase-0 TOML-filter port never happened, and it was tracked despite being gitignored — `.gitignore`'s `/vendor/rtk/` line goes too, and `.gitguardian.yaml` keeps its `vendor/rtk/filters/ssh.toml` skip entry (reworded: the path is history-only, `scripts/mirror/sync.sh --check` still scans it against the mirrored history) since deleting the entry would turn the mirror check red. yi-context drops five test-only items with proven production duplicates: `assemble`/`StablePrefix` (the environment hook already does this), `WorldState`/`WorldStateSection` (same hook, no implementer anywhere), `HarnessState` (the Python kernel owns the harness file, no Rust reader), `own_and_total_usage` (`yi-session`'s state already sums usage), and `Window::claim_advisory` plus its `advisory_delivered` field (no context advisory exists). `scripts/guardrails/check_manifests.py`'s `yi-cli` feature allowlist shrinks to `{"default", "tui"}`, its only declared features, and the script now errors on a stale allowlisted-but-undeclared entry the way `check_boundaries.py` already does. `crates/runtime/src/schedule/lanes.rs` drops its `catch_unwind` wrapper: in dist `panic = "abort"` makes it unreachable, and in dev/test `spawn_blocking` already turns a panicked `DeliverFn` into a `JoinError` that `reap_lane` logs and releases the lane's claims for the next `Scheduler::start` recovery. The no-TTY banner in `crates/cli/src/main.rs` no longer enumerates a stale, incomplete verb list ("more surfaces land in later phases" against 22 real verbs); it names only what makes sense without a TTY. |
| 0.323.0 | 2026-09-26 | An interrupt answers every tool call it leaves unrun and sends no further request (D245; Closes #530, Refs #534). Esc during the first of three sequential calls left the other two with no result in the session file, then the loop sent one more billed provider request (and ran a due compaction's summarizer) only to discard it; now each unrun call gets `Operation aborted` with its start and end events, and the run settles as the same empty `Aborted` turn without a request. `SoftInterruptQueue` and `synthesize_skipped`, the yi-loop half of #534, had no caller and are deleted. |
| 0.322.0 | 2026-09-26 | yi-session's two serialized output shapes live in yi-types (Closes #532). `SessionMetadata` (`yi sessions --json list/show`) and `SessionStats` (rpc `get_session_stats`) move into `yi_types::wire`, re-exported from `yi_session::query` so every call site resolves unchanged; `HistoryHit` and `LanePointer` drop a `Serialize` derive nothing used, and yi-session drops its `serde` dependency. `check_manifests.py` now fails `serde`/`serde_derive` in any non-`yi-types` crate's `[dependencies]`; YI_DESIGN.md §20 drops the query.rs serde exception (nothing outside yi-types derives Serialize any more), §18.3's `serde` row narrows its "Used by" column to `types`, and §21's `check_manifests` row names the new check. `crates/types/tests/session_output.rs` pins both shapes to bytes captured from the 0.309.0 binary. |
| 0.321.0 | 2026-09-26 | The workspace's `thiserror`, `lexopt` and `vt100` entries set `default-features = false`, restoring D31 and .ruler/060-deps.md's "default-features = false everywhere" (Closes #533). thiserror keeps `features = ["std"]`, the only default it draws (`AsDisplay` for `Path`/`PathBuf`, which `yi-runtime`'s `#[error]` attributes need); lexopt and vt100 have no `[features]` table, so the change only brings their manifest entries into line with the rule. `scripts/guardrails/check_manifests.py` now reads the root `[workspace.dependencies]` table and fails any non-path entry missing `default-features = false`, naming the dep. `Cargo.lock`, the dist binary (flipping the manifest in place rebuilds nothing and relinks byte-for-byte) and the dependency count are unchanged. |
| 0.320.0 | 2026-09-26 | The dist binary budget is a measured ratchet under a hard cap of 8 MiB (D249; Closes #536). `check_binary_size.py` gains `HARD_CAP_BYTES` (8,388,608), checked independently of `binary_size_budget.json`, so the baseline can never again be edited past it; PR #436 had raised the baseline to exactly 7 MiB by hand in an empty-body commit, which hid 865,664 bytes of growth across 20 PRs with no ratchet commit, and #545's measured ratchets have since taken it to 7,540,816. `binary_size_budget.json` stays at the measured 7,540,816 bytes and `deps_budget.json` moves to the measured 19 direct / 167 transitive; `.ruler/060-deps.md` and YI_DESIGN.md §18.6 state ≤ 8 MiB and the measured counts. `just push` ratchets only a lone budget overrun: a cap breach, a missing binary or any other failing check stops the lane with the check's message (`forge_pr.py --selfcheck` feeds it a cap FAIL). `check_binary_size.py --selfcheck` proves the cap fires for its own reason, independent of the baseline. |
| 0.319.0 | 2026-09-24 | Landed plans leave docs/plans (Closes #540). Nineteen fully landed plans and two evidence documents move to docs/archive/plans, each landed plan headed by the D-rows and versions that landed it; the nine that stay carry an `Open:` line naming what is left, checked against the code. docs/solutions/architecture.md becomes a one-page map into the design doc, and the solutions preamble, coding practices, README, FORGE.md, forge-planning and the two ledgers' preambles are brought in line with the code; every path to a moved plan follows it. |
| 0.318.0 | 2026-09-24 | ARCHITECTURE.md is the map of Yi as built (Closes #539). The header is one current-state line; the crate table lists all 16 crates with internal deps from boundaries.toml and budgets from the enforced ceilings; the feature ledger keeps the 36 shipped rows, each naming its journey test, and drops open, deferred and cut ones; the phase gates and review axes are gone; D-rows are untouched. The .ruler facts follow (sixteen crates, no phase gate, the loop's one `Result`), and code comments no longer cite other agents or ref/ spans. |
| 0.317.0 | 2026-09-24 | The design doc states what Yi is, organized by primitive (D243; Closes #527). docs/YI_DESIGN.md goes from 2,248 to under 900 lines: scope, crates, a primitive and composition table, then one section per primitive (session, provider, prompt, tool, permission, kernel, url, child, mailbox, plan, lane, goal and schedule, advisor), the surfaces, and the dependency, style, schema and guardrail law, each with its invariants, owning paths, state as data, yi-types shapes and settling D-rows. Every claim of the old doc was checked against the code; what the code does not do is gone, and nothing cites another agent or a ref/ span. Benchmarks and eval gates move to evals/README.md, memory to docs/memory.md; Appendix A, `.ruler/070-refs.md` and the yi-port skill are deleted; every section reference in the tree moves to the new numbers. |
| 0.316.0 | 2026-09-24 | Six console chat-pane defects close (D244; Closes #544). A streaming table is never cut through its body, so rows past half the pane no longer reach scrollback as raw pipes; a pane scrolled above the bottom holds still while the turn writes below it, and only the bottom or a sent prompt follows the stream again, where every streamed event used to snap the view down; a run of grouped reads commits above the reasoning that follows it; todo rows sit flush under the HUD inset; the full sidebar fits its longest name, capped at the old 20 columns; and colour reaches what stayed plain: a call's path splits into dim directories and its file name, bare paths and URLs in prose take their own hue, diff fences colour, Rust type names and calls colour by shape, and TOML, TypeScript, Swift, Kotlin and Dockerfile fences get real grammars. growth +433: two scroll and commit guards, the shape inference, the prose path marker and a per-cell render cache, each pinned by a test that fails on the tree before it. Review fix: a held pane anchors on the rendered rows below one pinned cell, where counting committed rows drifted a row per card and per thought paragraph, and holds its offset across a resize; cells render once per width and mode and only as far back as the view reaches, so a steady scrolled paint costs 0.3 ms near the bottom and 2.5 ms three thousand rows up; a table whose header has no leading pipe or whose rule is centred keeps it, a pipe-less row stays in its table, text after a table still commits, and a cut at a table's head keeps the blank above it; only a sent prompt returns a pane to the bottom, not a Shift+Enter newline; the sidebar never narrows within a run and fits workspace names; todo rows indent under their title beside a goal; and TSX identifiers, a fetch URL, a path split across `~` or `[`, `Node.js` and code-fence lines colour as intended. |
| 0.313.0 | 2026-09-24 | A request to a Claude model keeps its newest 20 images and at most 24 MB of image base64, and names each older one it leaves out (Closes #538). Past 20 images the Claude API holds every image in the request to 2000 px, and a request is capped at 32 MB; resent history counts toward both, so an image-heavy session was refused on every later turn. `transform::keep_newest_images` runs for the anthropic-messages API and for any model id naming claude (a Claude model through OpenRouter), replacing each older image with `(earlier image omitted: <mime>, <n> KB of base64; …)`; the host has no image decoder, so it drops rather than shrinks. Other providers are untouched: native OpenAI takes 512 MB and 1,500 images a request. Follows the provider-limit image caps of #522. |
| 0.312.0 | 2026-09-24 | The family board belongs to the root session (D242; Closes #516): a `--continue` finds what `rlm.put` left, and two sessions in one `yi acp` worker no longer share a board. `yi ask` and the TUI now settle their session id before building the session, so the board is keyed `<sessions>/family/<session-id>`; `/new` keeps the first session's board. Stage 10 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. Review fix: `yi sessions rm <id>` removes that session's board with it, refusing an id that fails session-id validation. |
| 0.311.0 | 2026-09-24 | A variable over the per-variable snapshot cap is serialized once, not on every checkpoint (Closes #514). The snapshot cell remembers each over-cap name by the id, type and `sys.getsizeof` of its value on the shell (`_yi_snapshot_over_cap`) and reports it skipped without dumping it again until the name is rebound or the object resizes; before, every debounced checkpoint dilled it up to the cap and threw the bytes away, holding the kernel for that long after each cell. Stage 8 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. Review fix: the post-compaction prune never trusts the memo, since a list, dict or instance edited in place keeps its id, type and size and the prune deleted a variable that had shrunk under the cap; a name leaves the memo when its dump succeeds or it is gone, and an in-place shrink outside a prune still stays skipped until rebound. |
| 0.310.0 | 2026-09-24 | The kernel's `bash()` runs inside the kernel's sandbox (D241; Closes #512). On macOS a job the kernel starts can no longer write outside the kernel's roots or reach the network, where before `await bash(...)` ran on the host unconfined. Stage 6 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. Review fix: a `bash()` job gets the kernel's loopback grant too, so a local server or test suite it starts can bind 127.0.0.1 again, while outbound traffic stays denied. |
| 0.309.0 | 2026-09-24 | A sandboxed child kernel can write its family board (D240; Closes #510). On macOS a child's `rlm.put` and a `kernel://<child>/var` object dump failed with EPERM, because the board sits beside the child's `sub-*` root, not under it. Stage 4 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. Review fix: the host no longer follows a link a child plants on the board — a spilled reply is written to a fresh file and renamed into place, and `family://` reads and `kernel://` object dumps refuse anything but a regular file. |
| 0.308.0 | 2026-09-24 | An image a kernel cell attaches reaches the model (Closes #515). `cell_output` appends a `Content::Image` block for each `image/*` attachment, which the provider mappers already carry in a tool result (a follow-up image message on openai-completions, and only for a model that takes images); before, it reached only `details.attachmentMedia`, while `attach_image` and two read hints said it was put in front of the model. Stage 9 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. Review fix: only png, jpeg, gif and webp up to `attach_image`'s 350,000 base64 chars become an image block; any other image attachment is named in a text note instead, since a block the provider refuses would fail every later request. Review fix: an image sent to the model is stored once; `details.attachmentMedia` keeps its mime type and path without the bytes, ACP tool-call updates carry the image block, and the console notebook reads it there, still accepting the bytes from an older details record. Review fix: each provider mapper has a request test for a tool result holding an image (anthropic inside `tool_result`, openai-completions a follow-up user message or a placeholder for a text-only model, openai-responses an `input_image` in the `function_call_output`). Review fix: the context estimate for an image rises from 4,800 to 7,680 chars, the 1920 tokens Anthropic charges for the 1200x1200 `attach_image` allows. Owner decision: images go to the model up to the provider's own limits, not a self-imposed cap: `attach_image` sends up to 10 MB of base64 and 8000 px a side (the Claude API's per-image limits), its 20 MB and 36 MP source caps are gone (Pillow's decompression-bomb check stays), `cell_output` passes an image up to 10 MB, and an image is estimated at the high-resolution tier's 4784 visual tokens. |
| 0.307.0 | 2026-09-24 | The kernel keeps no IPython history, and its ipykernel is pinned to the major Yi's host bridge was written against (Closes #513). The launch passes `--HistoryManager.enabled=False`, so no cell, Yi's internal ones included, lands in the user's `~/.ipython/profile_default/history.sqlite` or contends on its lock; `IPYKERNEL_REQUIREMENT` is `ipykernel>=7,<8` (each venv rebuilds once, since the requirement is part of its identity); and `_install_control_comm_handlers` raises, naming the missing internal, where it returned silently and left every host reply undelivered. Stage 7 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. Review fix: the missing-comm-manager error names `YI_KERNEL_PYTHON`, the only way to reach it, and `python/yi_runtime/pyproject.toml` pins `ipykernel>=7,<8` too. Review fix: `test_rlm.ControlCommTests` pins both branches: a kernel lacking the comm internals raises naming `YI_KERNEL_PYTHON`, and a shell with no kernel returns quietly. |
| 0.306.0 | 2026-09-24 | One process owns a session's kernel snapshot (D239; Closes #511). A second yi process continuing the same session boots its kernel empty and saves nothing, instead of reviving the first's names and overwriting its file; stderr says `another yi process holds this session's kernel state`. Stage 5 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. Review fix: a prewarm claims the lock too, since an unlocked prewarm's bootstrap snapshot let a later process take the session and the first then flushed over it; a refused kernel retries at its next boot, so it takes over once the owner exits; a filesystem that cannot lock counts as owned, with no message; a rekeyed kernel flushes before its old lock drops; and `rpc_protocol` picks the per-cwd directory, since the sessions dir also holds flat snapshot files. |
| 0.305.0 | 2026-09-24 | A `context_keys` value longer than the child's cap arrives marked `… [truncated to 4096 chars]`, never silently cut (Closes #509). `_resolve_context` cut each value at exactly `CONTEXT_VALUE_CAP`, so `mailbox::context_block`, which marks only a value longer than the cap, never fired; the kernel now sends one char past it. Stage 3 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. |
| 0.304.0 | 2026-09-24 | A file another process reads is replaced whole, never rewritten in place (Closes #508). `rlm.put` writes its object and its JSON sidecar through `_stage`, a per-process tmp and `os.replace`, so a put that fails midway (ENOSPC) leaves the previous sidecar parseable; the snapshot's data tmp is per-process and its manifest is renamed into place; the `kernel://` object dump writes a tmp and renames it. The mail reply spill is left as is: its name is unique per message and its reference goes out only after the write. Stage 2 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. Review fix: `put` stages both files before renaming either, so a failed put leaves `get` on the old object as well as `ls` on the old sidecar; the snapshot's data write removes its tmp on an interrupt too and re-raises it. Review fix: every tmp these writes stage (the put's object and sidecar, the `kernel://` dump, the snapshot's data and manifest) is removed if stale and created `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` at 0600, so a symlink a family member plants at the predictable name is never written through; tests hold the old manifest and dump open across a rewrite and see their inodes change. |
| 0.303.0 | 2026-09-24 | A `kernel://` read of an agent that is running a cell gives up at its 5 s deadline with `VariableReadError::Busy` ("the agent is running a cell; nothing was read within 5 s") instead of waiting out the whole cell, up to the 600 s ceiling, and failing with an empty detail (Closes #507). `KernelManager::execute` races the execution-queue lock against the call's abort, and `KernelService::variable_cell` starts its timer before the manager lock a boot holds. Stage 1 of `docs/plans/2026-09-24-kernel-defect-fixes.md`. growth +10895: the +10874 main carries for D238 against the 0.273.0 baseline, plus 21 src lines for the raced locks and the `Busy` variant. Weighed and left out: a `Busy` field naming the agent (the fetch error already names the variable) and a stale-value fallback. Review fix: `Busy` now means only that the read never reached the kernel (the queue or a boot held it); a read that ran and whose own `repr` outlived the deadline says `the read timed out after 5 s`, and the busy test waits for the sleeper cell to start instead of for a second. Review fix: `AbortFlag::fired` registers for the wakeup before it checks the flag, so a `fire` landing between the two can no longer be missed; no test pins it, since a 20,000-round stress race never lost the wakeup on the old code either. |
| 0.302.0 | 2026-09-24 | The kernel installs a pinned, verified uv instead of piping astral's install script into `sh` (D238; Closes #505). On a machine with neither uv nor python3 3.11+ the first boot downloads uv 0.12.18 for its target, refuses it unless the archive's sha256 matches the one compiled in, takes only the `uv` executable out of it and publishes it at `~/.yi/uv/0.12.18/uv`, which later boots reuse offline. `YI_INSTALL_UV` is gone. A report-only `yi doctor` names that fetch without running it; `--fix` runs it. growth +10874: D238 adds about 280 src lines, its tests inside them, over the +10595 main carries (the installer module and the ladder rung). Weighed and left out: a version self-check after extraction (the digest already pins the bytes), an install record or lock (the rename publishes atomically, so presence is the record), and #422's environment record. |
| 0.301.0 | 2026-09-24 | Native `yi login` / `yi logout`, with no provider identity in the binary (D191, Closes #228). `yi-oauth` is PKCE, the loopback, a 0600 token store (atomic tmp+rename, the mode set at creation) and the verbs; an OAuth provider is described by a file the user writes at `~/.yi/oauth/<provider>.json` carrying the client id, endpoints, redirect, `stream_headers` and `refresh_headers`. Yi ships no such file and no client id: `yi login <provider>` without one names the path and the example. `openai`/`openrouter`/`google` are pasted keys via `--key` or stdin. An env key still wins; a stored OAuth token refreshes before use. Profile headers reach the wire through D170's merge, so a name replaces and an empty value removes. `openai-codex` (ChatGPT's Codex backend) and `google` join the catalog. `scripts/oauth-profile-gen.py` writes a profile: plumbing done, `discover_*` returns `REPLACE_ME` and exits 1 naming every hole. A stored OAuth token is now re-resolved when it expires, not once per session: `ProviderStream` holds the credential behind a mutex with its expiry, and `key()` refreshes through the store's cross-process lock before a turn streams on a dead token, so a days-long TUI, ACP or daemon session stays logged in. The refresh rides the session's proxy (config and `HTTPS_PROXY`), not a bare client. A 401 against an OAuth credential ends with `run: yi login <provider>` in all three adapters. For `openai-codex` the account id comes from the id_token's JWT `https://api.openai.com/auth` claim (where codex-rs reads it), the `originator: yi` header always rides, and the system prompt goes once, in `instructions`. A profile file that exists but does not parse is reported broken with its reason, never silently "missing", and a profile carrying a key the schema does not know is refused. The token and profile files are typed in `yi-types` (versioned, with an `extra` map so a field a newer Yi wrote survives a rewrite) behind a `thiserror` `Error`; expiry is `SystemTime` inside, milliseconds at the file. The refresh lock is an OS file lock the kernel drops with a dead holder, so no age-based break can let two refreshes race a rotating refresh token; a waiter gives up after 50 s, past the slowest refresh (40 s), and the lock filename is sanitized. The ChatGPT account id is read from the id_token claim by key, not a JSON pointer, a newer refresh response's account and org win over the stored ones, a server `expires_in` too large for the clock means no expiry rather than a panic, and a profile's `redirect_host` must be loopback. PKCE and the URL helpers live once in `yi-oauth` and `yi-mcp-cli` reuses them; `https_or_local` matches the loopback host exactly, so `localhost.attacker.example` no longer passes. The dead device-code path is deleted, `yi logout` scans the token directory so a profile-less token still goes, and the loopback names a denial (`error=access_denied`) and checks the callback path. Tests: `oauth/tests/flow.rs` drives refresh, rotation, stale-lock break, concurrent single-flight, state mismatch and denial against a loopback token endpoint; `cli_surfaces::login_logout_round_trip` pipes a key on stdin and checks the 0600 file. growth +10595: D191 adds about 1495 src lines over the +9100 main carries against the 0.273.0 baseline (the `yi-oauth` crate, the auth resolve and its expiry re-resolve, the two catalog packs, the profile-header seam). Weighed and left out: shipping any client id or wire identity, a discovery script for them, TUI `/login`, darwin keychain, reading another client's token files, and login-only providers Yi cannot stream. |
| 0.300.0 | 2026-09-23 | A session's cached prefix stays put, and the live lane stops depending on OpenRouter's upstream draw (D231; Closes #495, Closes #480). growth +9100: measured against the 0.273.0 `src_loc.json` baseline main carries (D195), so the landed stack's growth rides inside it, of which this stage is -32: the leaked description cache, its mutex and the nonce generator go, and the fence id reuses the hash fetched content is already fenced by; keeping a per-session nonce and moving it into the environment block was weighed and refused, since a fence whose id is only checkable in another message proves nothing the hash does not. In a fresh HOME the kernel venv lands while the first session runs, and `read`'s description then gained its document sentence between two requests, so the tool table and everything after it went uncached: the forge lane's `bash-marker` read 0 cached tokens in almost every record. The description is now decided the first time a session sends its tools and held; a document read after the venv lands still converts. The yard's fences carried a random per-session nonce ahead of the tools, so a new session reused at most 6.4k to 8.2k tokens of another's prefix on upstreams that render tools after the system message; each fence is now named by the hash of the text it fences, which that text cannot contain, as fetched content already was. The live lane's verdict also hung on the upstream: Relace drops the whole cache on a prefix a few tokens past a 256-token page. The three flash workflows pin DeepInfra through `EVAL_ROUTING`, `run.json` records its `mode`, and the ledger's baseline reads only runs of the same model and mode, so the pinned lane rebuilds its history from empty and no threshold moves. Paired live runs pinned to Sail Research, two per binary: `bash-marker`'s second request read 7,168 cached tokens before and 12,800 after, every scenario's first request 6,656 before and 12,800 after, hit rate 0.81 and 0.82 before, 0.98 and 0.98 after. Seen red: `yi-tools documents::the_tool_table_holds_still_when_the_venv_lands_mid_session` (the table changed when the venv landed) and `request_budget::two_fresh_sessions_send_the_same_system_prompt_and_tools` (the yard's nonce differed). The ratchets stop speaking for one draw (#480): unchanged main had tripped ttft in 4 of 12 runs and the cache ratchet in 2 of 12. A ratchet now holds only when every run of the head that measured it crossed it, the PR lane running the suite again, three runs at most, while one does; its bound is the median's ratchet or the history's worst run, whichever is further; and the cache ratchet reads `cache-warm`'s warm turns, since the whole-run rate moves with how many cold requests the model takes. No threshold moved. Replayed over the telemetry branch, the old rule is red on 50 of 106 records and the new one on 7 windows of three, each a shift all three runs shared; over the 2026-09-23 matrix it is green on every triple of main, #427 and #483 and red on #467's three Relace misses. Seen red: the `live_ledger` selfcheck, against a judge of the first run only ("a ratchet one run held is noise"), a bound without the band, and the whole-run rate, and a later run with no number for a ratchet counted as one that held. |
| 0.299.0 | 2026-09-24 | Tool ergonomics and harness fixes from the confirmation corpora: the edit tool reads a one-mark near-miss hunk header and names a line past the end, the kernel keeps its namespace across a cancelled cell and says what a restart lost, the first cell shows `rlm`'s signatures, old venvs are swept, and the mined errors are split by who is to blame (D237; Closes #481, #501, #502). Edit: `PUT 40.:=40:`, the largest tool-attributable edit refusal left in #481's slice, applies as `PUT 40.=40:` with a warning naming the repair when every one-mark repair names the same op, an ambiguous line is still refused, and only `PUT` and `CUT` are repaired, so a bare body row `REM.` stays a row rather than deleting the file (`hashline_patcher::a_one_mark_near_miss_header_applies_and_names_the_repair`, `hashline_patcher::an_ambiguous_near_miss_header_is_still_refused`, `hashline_patcher::a_near_miss_never_reads_as_a_file_op`); all four `never displayed` refusals in the three confirmation corpora cited lines past the end of a file the model had just written whole, so the seen-lines guard leaves those to the range check, which says the file has N lines (`hashline_patcher::an_anchor_past_the_end_is_not_called_unseen`); the carry of seen lines across an edit was already in place (#473). Kernel: ipykernel queued an interrupt's cancel on the loop without waking it, so a cell polling `await asyncio.sleep(30)` outlived the busy window and was killed with the namespace; the bootstrap cell lets SIGINT wake the loop (`kernel_lane::a_cancelled_awaiting_cell_leaves_the_namespace_standing`), and a restart that still happens lists the names the restored kernel lacks, with `restarted; N names lost` on the environment block's `kernel:` line until the next cell (`kernel_lane::a_forced_restart_names_what_it_lost_in_the_result_and_the_state`). Surface: until the first cell the `kernel:` line names what `rlm` and `yi` offer and that `help(rlm)` and `help(yi)` are exact, and the first cell's result carries one row per public `rlm` function, once per session, both after the cache boundary, so the request budget is unchanged (`kernel_lane::the_first_cell_shows_the_rlm_table_once_and_the_state_names_the_surface`). Harness: a boot removes `~/.yi/kernel-venv-*` beside the current one when its build lock is free, no running process started from it and no boot has used it for a day, since every ready boot stamps its venv, so a second installed yi version is not rebuilt on each switch (`bootstrap_lock::a_boot_sweeps_only_the_venvs_nothing_uses`, `bootstrap::tests::a_ready_boot_keeps_its_venv_out_of_the_sweep`); parallel first boots unpack `~/.yi/python` once under its lock, where kernel_e2e on a fresh HOME had gone 4 of 6 flaky (`bootstrap_lock::parallel_first_boots_share_one_unpacked_runtime`); quitting the TUI or a headless drive during a running cell aborts the turn and bounds the runtime's wind-down to 3 s (`tui_drive::quitting_during_a_running_kernel_cell_exits_promptly`); the sandbox escape probe writes where no writable root reaches, not under a HOME that may sit under tmp. Mining: `extract.py` gives each failed tool result an `errorClass`, `tool`, `command`, `api_misuse` or `other`, from deterministic rules on the result text and details, checked in its selfcheck against 33 labelled rows from the corpora; the selfcheck moved to `selfcheck.py`, taking `extract.py` from 1,282 lines to 1,029, under the 1,200 cap. growth +9108: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +323 (runtime +127, kernel +139, tools +52, tui +5) for D237; weighed for deletion and taken: the bootstrap cell and the venv lock moved to their own files rather than grown in place, since kernel.rs and bootstrap.rs sat at 1,160 and 1,196 lines, and eleven comments dropped to hold the comment volume at 3596; weighed and kept: sending SIGINT to the kernel's process group from Yi before a restart, not built, since ipykernel already signals itself and the lost interrupt was the loop never waking, not a signal never sent. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.298.0 | 2026-09-24 | The mailbox's leftovers close: progress is read, a todo label names a plan child, a stuck child wakes a wait, a follow-up wakes an idle session, repair drops crash-left staging, a run concludes once, and an accepted todo's notice quotes the checks that passed (D236, amends D214, D218, D230 and D235; Closes #500). Progress: a child's `progress` was inboxed and never read (audit FN4), so it is reported to its parent without waking it and presented at the parent's next turn, the newest per sender (`requests_e2e::progress_reaches_an_idle_parent_at_its_next_turn_coalesced`). Names: four corpus sends to `billing` failed with `no agent named "billing"; known children: <plan>/billing` (U4), so every rlm and mail call resolves a todo label to the one plan child ending in it and refuses an ambiguous one naming the candidates, and `rlm.status(name)` takes it too (`requests_e2e::a_todo_label_alone_names_a_plan_child_unless_it_is_ambiguous`, `test_rlm.test_status_names_a_plan_child_by_its_todo_label`). Stuck: a derived `stuck` never moved the epoch (V3), so `rlm.wait` moves it with cause `stuck` once per episode (`requests_e2e::a_child_going_stuck_wakes_a_wait`). Follow-ups: an ACP or RPC follow-up to an idle session queued with nothing to start it, and no decision row records a reason to keep Pi's queue-only follow-up, so it now starts the turn (`session_faux::a_follow_up_to_an_idle_session_starts_its_turn`). Repair: `yi plan repair` drops the staging of an attempt whose `integration_intent` has no `integration_prepared` after it and names each drop (`lanes::accept::repair_drops_the_staging_a_crash_left_before_prepare_and_names_it`). Conclusion: a late `AgentStart` claimed an already concluded run and ended it again, so a session counts its runs and `resume` claims only a run past the last conclusion (`finish::tests::a_late_start_of_a_concluded_run_concludes_nothing`). Evidence: in all eight G5 directed trials the owner re-ran the check the engine had just passed, so a passing command item keeps its command and the last 300 characters of its output with the cut named (`ItemLine.evidence`), `plan: accepted` quotes each passing item and says not to run them again, naming any item the threshold let through without a pass as not covered, and the doctrine says an accepted todo is verified, with the system prompt 10 bytes shorter (`finish::tests::an_accepted_notice_quotes_each_check_the_engine_passed`). growth +8809: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +301 (runtime +298, types +3) for D236; weighed for deletion and taken: a `progress` field in `status` beside presenting it, since presenting reuses the one queue, the process-wide integration lock in repair, since repair already holds the lease integration holds across the window, and six older comments condensed to hold the comment volume at 3600; weighed and kept: a host-side stuck latch beside the probe's, since the probe runs only with a plan and `rlm.wait` needs it without one. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.297.0 | 2026-09-24 | Every open request is answered or chased, every run ends with an answer, and an un-awaited `rlm` call runs at once (D235, amends D230, D232, D233 and D234; Closes #499). This is stage M4 of `docs/plans/2026-09-23-the-mailbox-delivers.md`, from a paid confirmation of M1-M3 at 9f29fa88 (three rounds of the eight `mbx-*` trials on `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`). Requests: a member whose turn ends with a request to it still open is steered once through its queue (`[host] request parent-1 from "parent" is still open ...`), and a second such ending sends that turn's final text as the reply with `answeredBy: "final_text"`, so `mbx-service`'s `rlm.request` no longer waits out 300 s on a service that answered in prose (`requests_e2e::a_request_left_open_is_chased_once_then_taken_from_the_final_text`). Asks: a plain `rlm.send` to a child with exactly one request open to its sender answers that request (`answered`), as `mbx-ask` round 3's parent tried four times (`requests_e2e::a_plain_send_answers_a_question_only_once_its_sender_was_shown_it`); `rlm.wait` returns at once with `state: "asks"` and cause `asks` while a child waits on its caller, and with `state: "settled"` and `finished` when nothing in the family is live (`requests_e2e::a_wait_returns_at_once_on_a_question_and_on_a_settled_family`), and the handle and todo helpers that wait on one child pause instead of spinning on either. Calls: every public `rlm` call returns an `RLMCall` task scheduled on the kernel loop when it is made, so `mbx-steer` round 1's un-awaited sends go out at the cell's next await, before the result they were meant to shape; the after-cell hook starts nothing and names only the calls nobody awaited, gathered, waited on or read (`test_rlm.test_mail_sent_unawaited_goes_out_before_an_awaited_result`, `test_rlm.test_only_a_call_nobody_took_is_left_to_report`, `kernel_data_surface::a_live_kernel_runs_each_rlm_call_once_awaited_or_not`). Deadline: when the wind-down stops a turn that ended on tool calls, one last turn runs with tool choice `none` (`LoopConfig::last_word`), so the run answers (`session_faux::a_deadline_ends_the_run_after_the_turn_in_flight`); `yi ask` follows a turn in flight for 90 s past `--deadline` for that answer, then stops the session and shuts its runtime down with a grace, so a cell looping on `rlm.wait` no longer outlives the deadline (`mbx-fanout`, a faux spin cell under `--deadline 30` ends at 31 s where it ran to the 200 s kill before); a run that ends without a final answer exits 1 with `error: the run ended without a final answer` (`ask::tests::a_steered_turn_past_the_deadline_ends_the_run`), and the leftovers line names services and open requests (`ask::tests::a_run_that_ends_with_a_live_child_names_it`). Receipts: a child's receipt carries `presented`, when its model reads the message (`in its running turn, at the next message boundary ...`, `when its next turn starts: it is idle and this message wakes nothing ...`) (`requests_e2e::a_receipt_says_when_its_message_is_presented`). Review fixes (Refs #499), from a read-only review of this stage and a paid rerun of the eight trials at 06a40d3e (11 of 12 passed): a plan child steered after its brief kept the chase turn's text as its product, so the harvest and the `Last answer:` line now read `answer_text`, which skips a chase turn (`finish::tests::a_chased_plan_child_keeps_its_briefs_answer`); `yi ask` ran up to 110 s past a `--deadline` its caller kills at, so a running tool is cancelled at the deadline less the last word's grace (the longest turn measured plus 10 s, capped at 90 s and an eighth of the budget) and `yi ask` follows only to the deadline less its shutdown reserve (`cli_surfaces::a_run_past_its_deadline_answers_and_exits_inside_it`, `deadline::tests::the_last_word_reserves_the_longest_turn_and_the_shutdown_capped`); an at-once `asks` or `settled` wait counted toward the repeat breaker and repeated hot, so each returns once, a repeat at the same state with nothing moved refuses naming it, and the one-child helpers pause on that refusal (`requests_e2e::a_wait_returns_at_once_on_a_question_and_on_a_settled_family`, `finish::tests::only_a_wait_on_a_live_family_counts_for_the_breaker`, `test_rlm.test_a_helper_pauses_on_a_repeat_the_host_refused`); a final text over 16 KiB was refused and dropped, so it is kept whole as `family://reply-<id>` and its head sent (`requests_e2e::a_final_text_over_the_body_cap_is_kept_whole_and_sent_by_ref`); a length stop or a `--json` provider error exited 1, so only a run with no assistant text at all exits 1, and under `--eval` a run with no final answer exits 0 with `{"type": "no_answer"}` (`ask::tests::only_a_run_with_no_text_at_all_exits_nonzero_and_never_under_eval`, `cli_surfaces::a_run_with_no_text_exits_one_except_under_eval`); a plain send meant as information was taken as an answer, so it answers only a question its sender was shown and otherwise carries a `hint` (`requests_e2e::a_plain_send_answers_a_question_only_once_its_sender_was_shown_it`); eager spawns broke a semaphore throttle and an interrupt in the after-cell hook left calls running unreported, so only mail calls are tasks and the hook cancels the rest on an interrupt (`test_rlm.test_a_semaphore_throttles_spawns_made_up_front`, `test_rlm.test_an_interrupt_after_the_cell_cancels_the_calls_left`); `LoopConfig::last_word` gets its own test (`turn_effort::a_stop_after_a_tool_call_asks_once_for_a_tool_free_last_word`); the faux provider's fixed 64-event channel dropped a long reply's end. From the rerun: models guessed the wait reply's shape five times, so `wait`, `status` and `receive` return `rlm.Reply`, a dict that reads by attribute and names its keys when unpacked or indexed (`test_rlm.test_a_wait_reply_reads_by_attribute_and_names_its_keys_when_misread`); a `done` naming no item closed the whole list or was refused for an item already done, so it lands on the one running item and names several (`todo_e2e::a_done_naming_no_item_lands_on_the_running_one`); and the eight `mbx-*` trials are regression fixtures in `evals/fixtures/surface/scenarios.json`, with the fanout brief asking for the exact word and the steer parent waiting for its child's first tool call. A final confirmation at 2f516615 (14 of 16) found four more (Refs #499): `rlm.status(name)` returned a one-item list, so a poll loop read `st.get("counter")` as None for 400 s, and it now returns that child's one entry and raises `KeyError` naming the children for an unknown name (`test_rlm.test_status_reads_the_member_list_and_filters_by_name`); the last word was cut 5 s into its answer, since its grace was measured off quick tool-call turns and shared `yi ask`'s 10 s, so the grace is the longest answer turn measured, floored at 30 s and capped, plus the shutdown on top (`deadline::tests::a_last_word_that_streams_for_twenty_seconds_lands_inside_the_deadline`); a model call still streaming when the last word was due ran on for 273 s and was aborted with no output, so `LoopConfig::last_word_due` cuts it and the tool-free turn follows (`deadline::tests::a_stream_still_running_when_the_last_word_is_due_is_cut_for_it`); and a spawn handle had no `state`, so `RLMSpawnHandle.state` reads it from the host (`test_rlm.test_a_handle_reads_its_state_from_the_host`, `kernel_data_surface::a_live_kernel_runs_each_rlm_call_once_awaited_or_not` in a real kernel). A pre-merge review of 06a40d3e..97cd7be2 found four more (Refs #499): a cell that read a question off `rlm.wait` and answered it with a plain send got a hint, since only a presented message counted as shown, so a wait or `rlm.status` reply that names the question marks it shown (`requests_e2e::a_plain_send_answers_a_question_its_sender_read_off_a_wait`); `rlm.followup` and `h.send` wrapped a second task, so an un-awaited one went out after the next awaited call, and they now await the unwrapped `send` (`test_rlm.test_a_followup_or_handle_send_goes_out_before_a_later_await`); a provider error the loop retried past still exited 1 in plain mode, so the exit follows the last stop (`ask::tests::a_provider_error_the_run_recovered_from_exits_zero`); and the runtime shutdown after an early answer waited up to half the remaining `--deadline` on a blocking request, so it keeps its 10 s grace (`ask::tests::the_runtime_shutdown_never_waits_past_its_grace`). A review of 97cd7be2..6e1e6e17 found `rlm.status(name)` and `h.result`'s own waits marking every child's question shown, so a plain send meant as information answered a question the model never read; only a question whose note the model's reply carried counts now, and the one-child helpers and `h.state` read `quiet` (`requests_e2e::only_a_question_a_read_reply_quoted_counts_as_shown`, `test_rlm.test_only_a_read_the_model_sees_marks_its_questions_shown`). growth +8423: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +817 (runtime +615, cli +142, loop +56, types +4) for D235; weighed for deletion and taken: a host-side `quiet` wait flag against the repeat breaker, since a pause in the three helper loops reads the same reply (`quiet` now only keeps a helper's read from showing a question), and every comment the change could do without, condensing older ones in the touched files, to hold the comment volume at 3600; weighed and kept: `LoopConfig::last_word` over a second `run_loop`, since a second loop would end `yi ask`'s follow on its first `agent_end`. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.296.0 | 2026-09-23 | The human sees a child's question and stall and answers the question from a reply box, and host notices stop reading as the user's (D234, amends D25, D205, D214 and D233; Closes #498). This is item 9 of the mailbox audit (V1, V2, G3), stage M3 of `docs/plans/2026-09-23-the-mailbox-delivers.md`, with seven M2 review fixes. Cards: `ChildUpdate.flag` carries `needs_you` or `stuck` with `rlm.status`'s note, so the TUI card reads `? Writer · needs you` over `asks writer-1: Which file name?` and a hint, `! Writer · stuck` over its reason, and a finished child woken again draws a live card and commits a second one (`asks::a_card_shows_the_question_the_stall_and_a_second_run`); the console's sidebar marks the child `?` or `!`. Reply box: with the asking child focused (alt-↓) the composer is titled `reply to writer: asks writer-1: …` and Enter sends the host's `answer`, the parent's `reply_to` reply marked as the human's, instead of prompting the root; the console sends the same through `_yi/child_answer` (`asks::the_reply_box_answers_a_childs_question`, the real loop through the drive harness, and a `scripts/tui_pty.py` run of the real binary). The rule: the first answer wins, and the other, the parent's `rlm.send` or the human's box, is refused with `writer-1 was already answered by the human; nothing was sent` (or `by "parent"`), which the human reads as a notice beside `you → writer (answering writer-1): notes.md` (`recursion_e2e::the_humans_answer_resolves_a_question_and_the_parents_is_refused`, `recursion_e2e::the_parents_answer_first_refuses_the_humans`). Transcript: ACP sends a user-role message the user did not type as a standard `agent_message` tagged `_meta.yi.hostNotice`, live and on replay (`acp_unit::a_host_notice_is_never_a_user_message`), and the TUI draws it, and a focused child's brief, as a flagged notice and presented mail as `request writer → parent`, live and on replay (`asks::a_replayed_notice_and_mail_name_their_source`). M2 review fixes: a closed comm aborts its host request task, so an abandoned `rlm.receive` stops taking mail (H1, `kernel_e2e::an_abandoned_host_request_stops_its_host_task`); `Run.settle` leaves an asking child's todo running (H2, `test_yi_plan::test_a_live_asking_child_keeps_its_todo_running`); `Todo.result` raises the question its own cell must answer (M1, `test_yi_plan::test_a_todo_whose_child_asks_raises_its_question`); a reply nobody waits on wakes nobody (M2) and a reply that resolves its request is the call's return and is never presented (L1, `Delivery::Answered`, `recursion_e2e::a_childs_question_is_a_request_its_parent_answers_with_one_call`); a child stalled only by the idle clock inside a running tool call still holds `yi ask` (M3, `plan::finish::tests::a_child_idle_inside_a_running_tool_still_holds_the_owner`); a parent's `cancel` ends an open ask (L2, `recursion_e2e::a_cancel_ends_a_childs_open_question`). `--faux` now works outside `--headless` on `yi tui`, `yi ask` and `--solo`, so the PTY harness can script a family. M3 review fixes: a draft answers the question open when it started, carried as its id in `Command::Answer` and `SubagentHost::answer`, so a steer typed before a child asks stays a prompt and an answer the parent beat is refused, never sent to the root (1, `asks::a_draft_answers_the_question_open_when_it_started`, and a `scripts/tui_pty.py` run); the card and the box show a child's oldest open question and an answer resolves the one it names (2, `mail::tests::the_card_shows_the_oldest_question_and_an_answer_resolves_the_one_it_names`); the human's answer carries `answeredBy: "human"` on its envelope, leaves a `human_answer` entry in the parent's store, and tells the parent at its next boundary without waking it (3, `recursion_e2e::the_humans_answer_resolves_a_question_and_the_parents_is_refused`); a host notice goes to ACP as an `agent_message` a stock client draws, a user message stored before D25 replays as the user's, and `yi rpc` prompts are the user's (4, `acp_unit::a_host_notice_is_never_a_user_message`, `asks::a_replayed_notice_and_mail_name_their_source`, `rpc_protocol::responds_per_command_streams_events_and_persists_v4`); `--faux` on the console road is refused by name (5, `tui_drive::a_cassette_is_refused_where_it_cannot_reach_the_model`); `Desk::answered` is keyed by request id (6, `mail::tests::a_late_answer_is_refused_by_the_question_it_names`); a child blocked inside a tool call reads as in it (7, `plan::finish::tests::an_asking_child_is_not_submitted`). growth +7616: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +547 (runtime +285, tui +156, acp +42, types +33, console +14, kernel +10, cli +7) for D234; weighed for deletion and taken: the answer's receipt line lives once in the runtime (`answer_told`) for the TUI and ACP, the deliver receipt comments that `Delivery`'s own doc now carries, and every other comment this change could do without, to hold the comment volume at 3600; weighed and kept: `publish_all` on a stall notice, since event-driven clients have no roster poll, and `Delivery::Answered`, since the receipt must say the reply was not presented. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |

Receiving: `rlm.receive(timeout)` blocks on the member's own queue, returns the queued envelopes in send order, and records them read in an `agent_message_read` entry, so no boundary and no restart presents them again (`recursion_e2e::a_sibling_receives_its_mail_instead_of_polling`, the `mbx-siblings` trial; `test_yi_mail::test_receive_waits_on_the_host_and_hands_back_the_envelopes`); `yi.mail.receive` is the same call. Checks: `goal::run_check` takes a required cwd and `run_check_at` goes; a child's check runs in its worktree or the session cwd, and a discovery's, a goal's and a probe's in the session cwd (`recursion_e2e::a_checked_childs_check_runs_in_the_session_cwd`, the `mbx-discovery` trial). KernelDeath: a service whose kernel dies under a cell ends that run as `FailClass::KernelDeath` and respawns with its pending mail moved (`recursion_e2e::a_service_whose_kernel_dies_respawns_with_its_pending_mail`, a real kernel). M1 review fixes: only the model's own bare `wait`, `result` and a `status` naming the member mark an ending read (`recursion_e2e::a_finish_the_parent_already_collected_is_not_presented`, roads `wait` and `status`); a reap cancels before it aborts (`recursion_e2e::a_reaped_child_starts_no_run_for_mail_queued_before_the_reap`); a wait counts for the repeat breaker only with a live child or respondent behind it (`plan::finish::tests::only_a_wait_on_a_live_family_counts_for_the_breaker`); `refold` claims an open record no reader concludes (`plan::finish::tests::a_lagged_watch_concludes_a_woken_run_it_never_saw_start`); and a handle keeps waiting on `still running` in any state (`test_rlm::test_a_finished_child_woken_again_is_waited_on_not_refused`). Not taken here: `receive` filters by sender or kind, and the TUI's view of an ask (item 9, stage M3). growth +7069: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +323 (runtime +254, cli +69) for D233; weighed for deletion and taken: `run_check_at`, `conclude`'s asking arm, `family::pending_question` and `state_from_records`' messages argument, `Cause::ended`'s asked flag, and every comment this change could do without, to hold the comment volume at 3600; weighed and kept: the leftovers report, since the silent exit is the finding, and `Desk::asking`, since `needs_you` must name the request it waits on. | 0.294.0 | 2026-09-23 | An `rlm` call the cell never awaits runs once after the cell, on one surface that the module generates (D232; Closes #492). This is item 8 of the mailbox audit (U2 and U3), the stage after D230 in the mailbox pull request stacked on #483. The kernel preamble binds `rlm` to the module itself and `fetch` and `bash` to its functions; `_RLMCallable`, whose `result` had lost `timeout=`, is deleted, and the missing-runtime placeholder answers every name from `__getattr__` instead of a list of four. `rlm/__init__.py::_public` lists a function in `__all__`, which now carries `status`, `service`, `put`, `get`, `ls` and `list_agents`, and wraps an `async def` in `_handed_out`; the handle methods `RLMSpawnHandle.result` and `send` and `BashHandle.tail`, `poll`, `kill` and `wait` take the same wrapper. Every call still returns its coroutine, as before this row; inside a kernel `_handed_out` also appends it to `_UNSETTLED` and registers `_settle` as IPython's `post_run_cell` hook, which empties the list after every cell and runs each coroutine that never started and that no task owns to completion on the kernel's loop, printing `rlm.status() was not awaited, so the cell got a coroutine object, not its value; it ran once after the cell and returned [...]`. So `rlm.send("beta", …)` as a statement sends exactly once, `print(rlm.status())` prints a coroutine object and, one line later, the value, and nothing that awaits, gathers or schedules a coroutine is touched. Review fix: the first version of this row chose coroutine or value from each call site's syntax (`_call_sites`, `_hands_on_coroutine`, `_complete`, `_either_way`, running value calls through `nest_asyncio`). A review reproduced on 3.11 to 3.14 that it ran `cs = [rlm.send(n, m) for n in ns]; await asyncio.gather(*cs)`, a semaphore helper, `c = rlm.status(); await c` and `await (a() if x else b())` eagerly and one at a time and then raised `TypeError`, returned a coroutine silently for a method call split over lines, and let a finished call under `gather` wait for another task's `rlm.wait` to return; the reader, its four functions and `EitherWayTests` (which ran with no event loop) are deleted. The cost: a coroutine stored in one cell and awaited in a later one has already run when the later cell awaits it, and that raises. The module docstring, which `help(rlm)` prints first, says so in three lines, and `help` marks every call `async`. `RUNTIME_READY_CHECK` drops its hand list of `rlm.rlm.*` names for `all(hasattr(rlm, n) for n in rlm.__all__)` and `not hasattr(rlm, 'rlm')`, which moves the venv's name and rebuilds it once. M2's `rlm.receive()` is written as `@_public async def receive(...)` and needs nothing else; without the decorator the surface test fails. Tests: `kernel_data_surface::a_live_kernel_runs_each_rlm_call_once_awaited_or_not` (T2) drives a real kernel against stub host handlers through a gathered list, a semaphore helper, a stored and a conditional await, a multi-line handle call awaited and not, two tasks under `gather` (the one whose call finished is not held by the other's two-second `rlm.wait`), a bare `rlm.send` (sent exactly once, after the gathered two) and `print(rlm.status())`, with no `never awaited` warning; `ipython_faux::an_unawaited_spawn_runs_once_after_the_cell_and_says_so` holds the coroutine, then the note, then the handle, and one spawn; `kernel_data_surface::every_rlm_name_the_model_reads_is_public_in_the_package` holds every `rlm.<name>` in the ipython description, identity and doctrine to `rlm.__all__`; `test_rlm.py` adds `AwaitLaterTests` (the same patterns and the two tasks on a real loop; both red on the first version) and `SurfaceTests` (every public function of the module is in `__all__`, none is left an unrecorded coroutine function, and `pydoc` renders each). growth +6746: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is -12 (the preamble's placeholder; the Python package, which the gate does not count, nets -32 at 1,059 lines after the 95-line `_RLMCallable` and the call-site reader went), and the stack of twenty-nine stages, D192 to D195, D210 to D230 and D232, stands at +18959 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`; weighed for deletion and taken: `_RLMCallable`, the placeholder's stubs and, in review, the call-site reader. |
| 0.293.0 | 2026-09-23 | The mailbox delivers every message in send order, and no run ends while a message it owes a turn waits (D230, amends D214 and D218, extends D192 and D225; Closes #493). This is stage M1 of `docs/plans/2026-09-23-the-mailbox-delivers.md`, which lands with it, taking recommendations 1, 2, 6 and 10 of the 2026-09-23 mailbox audit. Queue: a session's mail, host notices and steers wait in one queue in arrival order (`session/run.rs`), presented at every message boundary; `followup` only wakes an idle receiver, so BANANA sent plain and CHERRY sent with `followup=True` to a busy child both reach its next boundary in that order (`recursion_e2e::sends_to_a_busy_child_reach_its_next_boundary_in_send_order`, the `mbx-steer` trial), and a woken turn presents what waited before what woke it (`recursion_e2e::a_woken_turn_presents_what_waited_before_what_woke_it`, the `mbx-service` trial). Every way a run ends goes through one `settle` that, under the status lock every enqueue takes, starts the next turn when a waking message or a follow-up is owed: mail queued in the turn the repeat breaker ended starts its own turn (`recursion_e2e::mail_queued_as_a_run_stops_early_starts_the_next_turn`); an abort keeps follow-ups for the user's next turn, and a session winding down starts none. `heartbeat_hook`, `wake_idle_hook` and `notice_hook` are that one queue; `wake_idle_hook`'s retake task, `take_pending` and `run_handle` are deleted, the goal's continuation takes the heartbeat hook, and a respawned service adopts its predecessor's queue. An inboxed envelope with no presented copy in the transcript is queued again when the store is attached, once (`recursion_e2e::an_envelope_a_crash_left_unpresented_is_presented_once_after_it`). Runs: a run that starts on a concluded child record is another run; its status reads `running`, `wait` wakes on its end, a second notice carries its answer, and each run bills only its own turns (`recursion_e2e::a_woken_childs_turn_is_a_run_the_parent_can_wait_on`, the `mbx-ask` trial); `turn_ended` and `Service.reading` go, since the one resume reader concludes a service's woken turn too, and a watcher that lagged past a run's start claims the live run from its refold. Notices: a lifecycle notice for an ending the owner already read through `result`, `wait` or `status` is dropped unread at the next boundary and wakes nothing (`recursion_e2e::a_finish_the_parent_already_collected_is_not_presented`), and a finish that reaps its child says the verdict and the reaped product as one `plan_relevance` line (`plan::dispatch::tests::an_inline_childs_finish_is_accepted_without_an_owner_op`, `a_failed_childs_last_product_survives_the_reap`). Wait and the breaker: `rlm.wait` replies `causes` beside `changed`, every envelope up moves the epoch so a cell blocked in `wait` reads a child's second message (`recursion_e2e::wait_names_why_each_child_moved`), and the repeat breaker exempts a batch during which the host's count of family waits moved instead of parsing the cell (`loop_events::a_cell_the_host_saw_wait_is_never_a_repeat`, `a_batch_the_host_saw_no_wait_in_is_a_repeat_whatever_it_says`); the plan tool's `view` exemption goes with the parser. `concurrent_notices_preserve_updates_and_coalesce_wakes` now expects the two notices at the tool's boundary and one reply, where the old follow-up queue owed a third turn. Not taken here: a stuck transition moving the epoch, and the Python `rlm.wait` docstring naming `causes` (the `rlm` surface is stage M3's item 8, in flight). growth +6758: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +199 (the stack of twenty-eight stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D230, stands at +18971 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: `wake_idle_hook`'s retake task and `take_queued`, `take_pending`, `run_handle`, `turn_ended` and `Service.reading`, the loop's `waits` and `waits_only`, and every comment this change could do without, to hold the comment volume at 3600; weighed and kept: `adopt_pending`, since a respawn must not drop a steer or a follow-up that is not mail, and the `Cause` vocabulary, since a derived cause cannot tell mail from a live run. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.292.0 | 2026-09-22 | The five failure classes the final paid confirmation found are removed at their decision rather than reworded (D229, amends D226, extends D225 and D227; Closes #489). This is stage G5 of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. Eval road: the harbor adapter's `run_command` passes `--here`, as `evals/run.py` and `evals/surface.py` already do; `yi ask` keeps its lane, since D119 and `journeys::an_ask_in_a_repository_runs_on_a_lane_and_hands_it_back` pin a lane for a non-interactive ask in a repository, and without `--here` accepted work landed in lane 0 and never reached /app (`fan-out` and `fleet-forensics` scored 0, and 1.0 with it). The todo list: a plan opened over the owner's session list keeps the owner's own rows (`todo/mirror.rs::own`), ids and all, in their phases ahead of the plan's; a row whose label the plan declares is the plan's and keeps its id; while the plan is open the todo tool steps the owner's rows (stepped on their own and rejoined, so the plan's rows never move) and refuses the rest `Mirrored`, and a `todo start` or `done` on a plan item goes to the plan tool as the owner's op (`TodoStore::carry` over `mirror::carry`, set at depth 0 in `wire_plan_request`): a pending inline todo is started and then done, a delegated one reads its standing. Children: a child's plan tool has its own description and a schema whose `op` is `view` alone, and `tool.rs::declared` refuses a child's op other than `view` or `submit` with `ArgError::ChildViews` (`not_owner`) before normalizing or parsing, on the tool and on `plan.op`; every brief ends `When the work is done, end your turn with your answer; the engine takes it as your work.` (not "submits", since `a_brief_never_names_submit` pins the verb out of briefs). Finishes: `finish.rs::fail_and_retry` fails a candidate that conflicts with the parent (`MergeFailed`), retained, with cause `its candidate conflicts with the parent at <paths>: the next attempt starts from the parent's current tree`, and a verdict whose failed items include a schema item likewise, then retries the todo as `Actor::Engine` (`check_actor` admits `retry`) unless the engine retried it before; the re-dispatched child starts from the parent's current tree. The new `plan/brief.rs::lines` feeds `ensure_spawned`'s copy of the delegation, never journaled: a todo with an output schema or a contract `schema` item gets `Answer with only a JSON value matching the schema: no prose and no fence around it.` and the schema itself (`Schema: {...}`, or its `plan://` url past 2000 characters), and an attempt after a failed one gets `Your previous attempt failed: <cause>. Its transcript is history://<agent>.`. A child is not re-driven in place: a finished plan child's run ends at `conclude`, and the host concludes again only for a service. The finish hook, finding no todo running by the child, waits out the store lease once and looks again, so a child that ends before its start commits (the engine's retry spawns off the runtime thread) is still the engine's. Declaration: a worktree delegation with no contract and a stated accept is refused in `declare.rs` with `{"command": "<shell check>"}` as the road; the plan tool's todos description names `covers?: [glob]` and a `cmd` decider as `"shell command"`, and drops `from the plan's store`, so the request budget falls 47688 to 47669 bytes. Waiting: a bare `rlm.wait` (`SubagentHost::wait_for` with `named`) wakes on a move that names a child, or once nothing runs; a reap, the engine's after every finish, moved the epoch, named no one and woke it in 20 ms; a cursor-carrying wait, the library's, still wakes on it. The `plan-covered-write` surface scenario the confirmation added is ported. Tests, each seen red first: `todo_mirror::an_owners_open_items_survive_the_plan_and_the_todo_tool_steps_them` and `a_todo_done_on_an_inline_plan_item_is_carried_to_the_plan_tool` (T0), `a_plan_opened_through_the_tool_is_the_sessions_todo_list` now carries a todo `done` to the plan (T1); `plan::tool::a_childs_plan_tool_views_and_is_refused_before_the_parse` and `plan::dispatch::a_brief_never_names_submit`'s last line (T0); `lanes::accept::a_merge_conflict_at_the_finish_fails_the_attempt_and_the_engine_retries_it` (T1, replaces `a_merge_conflict_at_the_finish_is_named_by_view`: the conflict no longer leaves the todo); `plan::finish::a_prose_answer_to_a_schema_is_retried_once_with_the_schema_and_the_error` (T0); `plan_declare::a_stated_worktree_accept_is_refused_with_the_command_road` and `subagent::a_bare_wait_sleeps_through_a_reap_while_a_child_runs` (T0); `evals/selftest.py`'s `check_command` asserts `--here`. Rewritten to pin the new rules, never weakened: `todo_mirror::a_plan_op_replaces_the_list_and_keeps_ids` (the owner's `first` keeps `t1` in its own phase), `plan_e2e::a_child_stores_the_product_of_the_attempt_it_submits` (a child's `done` carrying a blob is refused for authority before its artifacts). Not built: a same-child follow-up on a schema refusal (the finished child's run is over; the retry names its transcript instead); an automatic retry on any other red verdict; the confirmation rerun, which is paid and user-run. The G5 review (Refs #489), each fix with a test seen red first: a removal keeps its name and last listed move in a bounded list (`Children::take`, 64 kept; a cursor older than a dropped entry never sleeps), so a bare `rlm.wait` whose cursor predates a child's end wakes and names it after the engine reaps it, where it read quiet and slept up to 300 s holding the `plan: accepted` line (`subagent::a_bare_wait_wakes_on_a_child_that_ended_and_was_reaped_unseen`, T0); the mirror and `resync` project from the list under the todo lock (`TodoStore::replace_with`), so an owner-row write between the read and the replace is no longer undone (`todo_mirror::a_plan_op_racing_an_owner_row_write_never_undoes_it`, T0); a retry whose start the engine refused (a spent fuse, a contract refusal, a spawn refusal) reports `the engine retried it, and the engine could not start ...` instead of `retried it once` (`plan::finish::a_retry_the_engine_could_not_start_says_so`, T0); the finish hook takes a child named under a plan the store holds and waits out the lease and looks it up inside its `spawn_blocking`, never with a sleep on the runtime thread the child's task runs on, and a child that still runs no todo gets `plan: "<name>" <verb>, and no todo runs by it` (`plan::finish::an_early_ends_lease_wait_leaves_the_runtime_running`, T0); `plan_e2e::a_child_stores_the_product_of_the_attempt_it_submits` adds a `view` carrying a blob, refused `only the plan owner stores artifacts` (seen red with `own_product`'s non-submit arm passing); while a plan is open the todo header names the first running plan row, and `normalize` starts no owner row, since its promotion keeps a standalone list's current item and the plan's rows are the running work (`todo_mirror::an_owner_row_is_not_promoted_to_running_while_the_plan_is_open`, T0; `a_plan_op_replaces_the_list_and_keeps_ids` now pins `running: gate`). only a child asking you something (`needs_you`) blocks its todo (`test_yi_plan::test_a_reaped_child_is_read_not_blocked`); the reviewer wall applies `deny_write` to what a shell command writes (redirect targets, an in-place `sed` or `perl`, the operands of `rm`, `mv`, `tee`, `touch`, `chmod` and kin, the destination of `cp`, `ln` or `install`, `dd of=`, a `git checkout`, `restore` or `rm`), so `python3 /app/check.py`, `sed -n` and `ls` of a write-walled standard run, and a refusal names the list that covers the path, `deny_write` or `deny_read` (`wall_e2e::a_bash_read_of_a_write_denied_path_runs_and_a_write_to_it_is_refused`, 11 refusals); the confirmation's logs show no record left live at the end, so the leak is not pinned to one holder, and the hold now counts only what wakes the owner); an owner `start`, `submit` or `done` on a delegated todo in a plan the engine closed at its last accept reads its standing (`done: the engine accepted it`) instead of `no plan is open` or `not active` (`declare.rs::owner_root`, `plan::finish::an_owner_done_after_the_engine_closed_the_plan_names_the_acceptance`); a `todo set`, `init` or `append` with a plan open writes the owner's rows beside the plan's, their ids minted past the plan's, where the prelude that fired before `plan init` led to a `Mirrored` refusal (`todo_mirror::a_todo_set_or_init_under_a_plan_writes_the_owners_rows_beside_it`); a todo id followed by its label, cut or the whole request line it was cut from, names its item (`todo_e2e::a_label_the_renderer_cut_names_its_item_back`).
