| 0.272.0 | 2026-09-20 | `fetch` pages: a capped read names the next offset (D213, extends D164; Closes #456). This is stage F1c of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11). The `fetch` host request takes `offset` and `limit`, and a paged reply carries `next_offset`, null at the end: bytes of a `local://` text, entries of a `history://` listing, chars of another member's `kernel://` repr, which is how a repr past the 8,192-char cap is read at all. A request that names neither key is answered key for key as before (`fetch_session::a_read_that_names_no_page_replies_exactly_as_it_did_before_paging` holds the reply's exact JSON), so no caller, tool description or prompt byte moves and the request budget is untouched. Edges, each decided and tested: a zero, negative, fractional or non-numeric `offset` or `limit` is refused at the host boundary and never clamped; a huge limit means the rest, except that a `kernel://` page is clamped to the read cell's `VARIABLE_MAX_CHARS` and names the next offset; an offset past the end is an empty last page with a null `next_offset`, so a loop on `next_offset` ends; a byte offset is the caller's arithmetic and can land inside a UTF-8 sequence, so both cuts move to a char boundary and the end always passes the offset (`an_offset_inside_a_character_still_serves_text_and_always_advances` tries every offset of a text with two, three and four byte characters at limits one to five: every page is text, every `next_offset` advances, and from zero the pages rebuild the file exactly). A page is refused where nothing stable counts it: on every other scheme, on one `history://` entry, and on `history://<agent>/tail/N`, a window anchored at the end of a growing listing, so any append slides it; the whole listing and `since/<seq>` are anchored at the start and page cleanly, and a paged read of the reading session's own history appends no fetch-log row (`FetchLog::remember`), so a walk of it ends at any limit instead of chasing the rows each page wrote (`fetch_session::a_walk_of_this_sessions_own_history_ends_at_a_limit_of_one`). The page's hash and its fetch-log row are the page's own; the log keeps one row per address and every reader of it is a membership test. `rlm.fetch(url, offset=, limit=)` returns `rlm.Page`, a `str` with `next_offset`, and sends only the keys it was given; a paged `kernel://` read is text, never the dilled object. `Resolver::fetch` is `fetch_page(url, None)`, `KernelVariables::read` and `KernelService::read_variable` take the page, and the reply is built by `Fetched::into_reply` so the unpaged shape is testable. Tests: `fetch_session::a_capped_read_names_the_next_offset_and_the_next_page_continues_it` (T1) and three edge tests beside it, the live-kernel page read inside `kernel_data_surface::a_live_kernel_fetches_its_own_variables_and_runs_bash_handles` (T2), and `test_rlm` for the payload. Reconciled against the plan: `roles.verify_quotes` still reads the whole page, because the digest it pins is the whole page's sha256 and a page's digest is not interchangeable with it in a stored quote; reading only the line's neighbourhood needs a digest rule of its own and is left as a follow-up the plan now names. Not built: paging for `tree://`, `family://`, `checkpoint://`, `plan://`, `agent://`, `user://` and `mcp://`, and a host-request journey through the real wiring (the handler is `Page::from_payload`, a refusal of a page named beside D164's `object`, `fetch_page` and `into_reply`). growth +12181: the stack of eight stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D213, of which this one adds 126 Rust `src` lines against 90 estimated (the trait and both resolvers had to carry the page, and the reply moved into `fetch/mod.rs` to be testable); weighed for deletion and taken: one `Page` serves all three units, the existing `VARIABLE_MAX_CHARS` slice in the read cell became the page's clamp rather than a second cap, and the live-kernel check rides an existing tier-2 journey. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.271.0 | 2026-09-20 | Two shapes ship as library schedulers under the engine's admission and step table: fork_join over isolated writers and readers, and scatter with readers bound to partitions and quotes verified against the archive; four more are specified with their prerequisites (D212, extends D211; Closes #455). This is stage F1b of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 8.5, 8.6 and 11). `python/yi_runtime/src/yi/shapes.py` (stdlib only): both shapes are module-level `async def`s over `(plan, run)`, so the lease's identity check attaches a second `plan.run(shape=fork_join)` to the first, and their levers are module constants (`MAX_RESTARTS` 3, `RESTART_WINDOW` 600 s, `SCATTER_MAX_ROUNDS` 3). Each checks geometry before its first `start` and raises `Geometry(problems)` naming every problem at once: fork_join wants at least two todos that can run side by side, each a `Writer` in its own worktree or a `Reader`, and refuses a writer or an inline todo in the shared workspace; scatter wants two or more readers on disjoint partitions (a url inside another's directory counts as shared), each with a `schema` item so its answer is stored, and exactly one pending inline lead. One loop serves both: start what is ready in plan order, stop at a start refused for `admission` and ask again after the next settle with nothing behind it tried first, settle, repeat. fork_join restarts one_for_one: a todo that ended `failed` is retried alone, at most `MAX_RESTARTS` times within `RESTART_WINDOW` across the shape, after which nothing new starts and what runs is settled; a `retry` the engine refuses (`retries_exhausted`, `illegal_step`) is final and stays in `run.refusals`, and a blocked todo is never retried because only `failed` is. scatter runs rounds: the readers answer `shapes.ANSWER` (`{answer, quotes}`, a null answer abstains), an abstention, a failed reader and an answer with no surviving quote are dropped, and the lead, `async def lead(answers, number)` declared as the plan's one inline todo, sees only `{reader, answer, quotes}` and returns `{"commit": answer}` or `{"ask": question}`; a commit is submitted as the lead todo's product `{answer, rounds}` and goes through `done`, a later round declares `<reader>-r<n>` todos with the reader's own delegation and contract, the question as the note and the key alone as the label (a `TodoLabel` is eighty characters and holds no newline, and the lead writes the question, so any question a real lead would ask refused the round-two declaration until the review caught it; the fake host holds the label rule now), and the last round without a commit fails the lead with the cause. `roles.verify_quotes` fetches each cited url once through `rlm.fetch`, keeps a quote whose text is on its line inside the reader's own partition, and pins the sha256 it was read at; provenance, not entailment. The partition arm is what makes the disjointness geometry mean anything after the first start: the reader's wall is cooperative (section 7.6) and the owner fetches with its own reach, so a quote citing another partition, or a page outside the archive, would otherwise verify. The walkthrough fixtures are programs under `python/yi_runtime/tests/programs` (`typo-fix`, `arc-game`, `campaign`), and `plan_walkthrough::a_program_and_its_json_fixture_reach_the_same_plan_json` runs each as a cell of a real kernel beside its JSON twin on a second engine and compares every plan's state and version, its todos in order with their states, edges, attempts, retries, whether they are delegated and what a done one output, and the committed transitions in journal order, while `refusals`, `touched`, cause and note text, and a delegation's or contract's contents are not compared; `kernel_data_surface::both_shapes_schedule_under_the_real_admission_and_step_table` runs both shapes against the real `admit`, step table, verifier and resolver with a width of one, where the journal shows each refused start asked again and never overtaken, a reader whose quotes cite a wrong line and another partition is dropped before the lead, and a reader whose child failed is retried and dropped, the two legal steps from `failed` to `abandoned`. That journey found two faults the fake host could not: the host's schema subset has no union type, so `ANSWER` leaves `answer` untyped, and a bare string product is stored as text a `schema` item finds no JSON in, so the commit is an object. `Todo.decompose` looked a sibling edge up in the parent plan and raised on its own docstring example; it resolves edges among its batch now. Overhead on the fake host, in host requests: fork_join over two writers 9 against 4 sent by hand, scatter over two readers and a lead 18 against 2 direct fetches; every extra request is a read or a wait. Reconciled against the plan: the section 6.3 cap counts refused verdicts per attempt (`done.rs` `refused_verdicts`) and the scheduler sends one `done` per attempt, so the durable bound on a retrying shape is `RETRY_CAP`, and section 6.3 step 6, which read as though the lifetime `todo.refusals` counter were the cap, now says the per-attempt rule that `plan_ops::a_retry_opens_a_fresh_refusal_count_so_only_retry_cap_bounds_a_scheduler` pins; the lead is a function, not an object with `commit`; `verify_quotes` reads the line from the whole url because the host's fragment needs a tag, which F1c's paged `fetch` is what makes cheap on a large page; the Python test ids carry unittest's `test_` prefix; two controls were added (the real-engine journey and the counted overhead). Not built: a writer child as lead, `rest_for_one`, `one_for_all`, the four later shapes, and the paired T3 row against direct retrieval, which is user-run. growth +12055: the stack of seven stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D212, of which this one adds no Rust `src` line (Python is outside the measure); weighed for deletion and taken: fork_join and scatter share one scheduling loop, the lead protocol is a return value rather than a class, and no restart strategy with a single caller got a type. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. Review fix: `fork_join` counted a `done` todo as one of the two that run side by side; it now counts ready and running todos only, so a rerun needs two arms left to run (Refs #455). Test: `test_yi_shapes::test_a_done_arm_is_not_one_of_the_two_that_run_side_by_side`; the one-for-one rerun declares a third writer. |
| 0.270.0 | 2026-09-20 | Plans are programs: the `yi` library opens, attaches to or resumes a plan, declares idempotent todos with contracts, runs one scheduler under a lease, and records its cells as an audit artifact (D211, extends D166; Closes #454). This is stage F1a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 8 and 11), the first user-space stage: F0 made the plan engine one host request with a journal, verified completion and worktree acceptance, and the kernel could drive it only through raw `rlm.plan_op` calls that answered in rendered text. Library (`python/yi_runtime/src/yi`, in the wheel the kernel already installs, stdlib only): `Plan.create(goal, request_id)` never adopts a plan by its text and a retried create is the same plan; `Plan.attach` reads a plan as it stands; `Plan.resume` reads durable state and the host's reconciliation findings, never saved source, and lists in `plan.unresolved` every running attempt nobody can vouch for, which the scheduler never retries, fails or restarts. `plan.todo(key, label, after, delegate, accept, run)` is idempotent by key: the same declaration writes nothing, a changed one raises `SpecDrift(key, diff)` and writes nothing, and the append carries the revision it compared against. `Todo` holds the ops (`start`, `submit`, `done` raising `Refused(verdict)` or `Stale`, `fail`, `block`, `unblock`, `retry`, `decompose`, `result`, which keeps waiting on a child that asked you something instead of raising, `cancel`); `contract`, `cmd`, `schema` and `example` build the yi-types contract with every criterion frozen by content when the todo is declared; `Writer` and `Reader` build the delegation and its wall. `plan.run(shape, budget, detach)` is one scheduler per plan under a lease the owner kernel holds: the same shape attaches, another raises `RunActive`, and `Run` ends `verified_success`, `accepted_by_user`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `unresolved` (a completion no contract verified is never reported as verified). The default shape starts what the host says is ready and retries an admission refusal after a slot frees; `Run.launch`, `Run.settle` and `Run.over` are the seam F1b's shapes drive. A child the host calls `stuck` is waited on, not failed, and one it cannot vouch for (`needs_you`, or a name `rlm.wait` no longer lists) blocks its todo on the user rather than leaving the scheduler to drop it; a finished child whose contract has a `schema` item has its answer stored as the product; a `done` refused by a verdict on the product fails the attempt with the verdict in the cause, while an abstention or an escalation leaves the attempt for a decision, since neither judged the product (section 6.3). Host: `Op::Program { cell_id, source_ref }` (`crates/runtime/src/plan/program.rs`) journals a cell's source, read back from the artifact store before the record names it, carries the sha256 `program.py` has once that cell is appended, appends `# --- cell <id> <iso-time>` and the source to `program.py` after the commit (the next record appends any cell a crash left out; nothing rewrites the file and nothing executes it), records a cell id once, and is legal on a done plan so a `retry` there can be recorded first. The library takes the cell text from IPython's `pre_run_cell` hook, which the kernel prelude installs by importing `yi`, and sends the record before the cell's first mutating op on each plan. `plan.op` now answers with the typed `plan` and `notices` beside the text, names the engine's refusal `kind` and a refused `done`'s `verdict`, and takes `artifacts` (`{media_type, text}` blobs, owner only, at most thirty-four of one MiB, stored under the store's own digest before the op applies), because until now only a test could put a criterion into a plan; `plan://<id>/artifacts/<sha256>` serves a stored blob, which is the host-minted address of an inline product. `rlm.plan_op` passes `artifacts` through, and the venv identity hashes every package under the wheel's `src`, not `rlm` alone, so an edit to `yi` rebuilds the venv (`crates/kernel/src/bootstrap.rs`). Gates: `help(yi)` is the documentation, `test_yi_help` checks every name in `yi.__all__` and every public method for a last-paragraph example whose names exist and whose awaits match each callee's kind, `ext_e2e::fragment_examples_name_real_kernel_apis` knows `yi.` and `plan.` and refuses a name that is gone, and `check_prompt_examples.py` derives its modules from `python/yi_runtime/src/*/__init__.py` and treats every `yi` class as a handle. No prompt bytes moved. Reconciled against the plan: a todo's key lives in its label (`key: label text`) because `TodoSpec` has no field for it and thirty-five construction sites; a builder's `local://` input is frozen at declaration, not at `start`; `programHash` is set on the `program` record alone; the scheduler lease is kernel-local until F2b puts leases in the store; the T0 source-record test is `plan_program::program_records_source_before_the_first_effect` because `store.rs` sits at its cap; the Python test ids carry unittest's `test_` prefix. Not built: a marker for a recorded cell that later raised, a token budget, and `shapes`, `mail`, `judge` and `recipes/`, which are F1b, F2a and F3a. growth +12055: the stack of six stages, D192 to D195, D210 and D211, of which this one is +346 (`plan/program.rs` at 181, the typed reply and the artifacts rider in `plan/request.rs` at 94, the artifact route in `fetch/schemes.rs`, and the op's arms); weighed for deletion and taken: the T0 test moved out of `src` into `tests/plan_program.rs`, a `key` field on `TodoSpec` and a second host request for artifacts were not added, and the library re-implements no admission, step or verdict rule. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. Review fix: the export found a cell on disk by searching all of `program.py` for its marker, so a source that quoted a later cell's marker line kept that cell out of the file while `program_hash` counted it; `export` now walks the file cell by cell over each cell's own bytes (Refs #454). Test: `plan_program::program_records_source_before_the_first_effect` records a cell whose source quotes the next cell's marker line. |
| 0.269.0 | 2026-09-20 | A child's removal, abort and every other exit publish one terminal update; the TUI and console reconcile against the roster, survive bus lag and no longer hold finished cards behind a live cell (D210, extends D165; Closes #453). This is stage F1e of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11), a bug fix from the child lifecycle audit that lands on its own evidence. A child's lifecycle lived in the host's record map, the event bus and each client's cache with no shared transition: `SubagentHost::delete` and `SubagentHost::reap` took the record away without a publish, and `run_child` then found no record and skipped its own terminal update, so a TUI card frozen at `Running` was never committed and never dropped, and `rlm.delete_subagent` before a child's first event minted one such card per call. Host: `SubagentHost::retire` (`crates/runtime/src/mailbox.rs`) is now the one way a record leaves the roster; `delete` and `reap` both call it, so `reap` gains `delete`'s refusal of a worktree with no recorded disposition, and the accepting road of `done` marks the published branch retained so the reap it runs is not refused, and the final `ChildUpdate` is built from the removed record with a cut-short run forced to `Error` and the cause `interrupted`. `run_child` judges a run by its last assistant message, maps an aborted stop to the same cause, tells the parent `[subagent … interrupted]` instead of `finished`, and once the record is gone publishes, notifies and bills nothing, because `retire` already spoke. A run carries the interrupt epoch read when it was requested (`AgentSession::prompt_requested`, `abort_epoch`), so an abort fired before admission is no longer cleared and a child deleted before its first poll never starts. `spawn` reserves the name and the slot under the roster lock and builds the child outside it (`crates/runtime/src/subagent/build.rs`), so `states` and `list` answer during a slow build; the host's watch re-reads its counters from the session after a lagged bus. Clients: `App::sync_children` ends a running card the roster no longer lists as `gone` and commits it; both TUI forwarders are one `forward` (`crates/tui/src/app/bridge.rs`) that reports a lagged bus as a notice and keeps going, as the ACP forwarder does; a finished card waits only on the kernel cell it was born under, so a lost cell end cannot hold every card; a card adopted after the first tool event names that tool from the child's transcript instead of drawing `starting`, and the record's answer preview outranks a folded stream that lost events. The console row cache evicts its oldest finished row at thirty-two rather than dropping the new one, and gives no row to a child first heard of at its end. Kernel side: `RLMSpawnHandle.result` raises on a `stuck` child with its note, `rlm.delete_subagent` takes the handle `rlm.run` returns, and the wait it passes the host is clamped to the asked timeout (a float ulp past it failed `test_result_uses_wait_and_keeps_its_errors` about one run in six). Reconciled against the plan: `retire` publishes for every removal and `run_child` stays silent on a missing record, which is what makes a child's own exit exactly one update, a removal after that exit repeating it rather than contradicting it; the console cannot tell a retired child from a retained one without a protocol change, so its rule is the first-seen-finished one above; the commit gate keeps the born-under ordering the `a_child_that_finishes_inside_its_spawning_cell_lands_under_it` pair test defends. The `deadline` road of the exit test is not built: a session deadline ends the turn after it settles (`should_stop_after_turn`), so a child that runs out of clock leaves by the `complete` road already covered, and `set_deadline` is crate-private. growth +11709: the stack of five stages, D192 to D195 and D210, of which this one is +344 (`subagent/build.rs` at 95, `retire`, the TUI's `app/children.rs` and `app/bridge.rs`, which are mostly moved out of `app.rs` to clear its cap, and the console row rule with its unit test); weighed for deletion and taken: the `list_subagents` poll was already gone at F0a, the TUI's two forwarder loops became one, and `delete` and `reap` lost their private removals. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.268.0 | 2026-09-14 | A worktree todo is done only when its candidate and its integration both pass and the acceptance is recorded; every other exit records a disposition and never merges to free a slot; a stuck child is reported once per episode (D195; Closes #426). This is stage F0d of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11, sections 6.6, 7.5 and 7.6). `crates/runtime/src/plan/acceptance.rs` is the integration coordinator: the child's `submit` settles its lane quiescent as the candidate, verifies that commit in a checkout from the verification reserve, prepares the merge in a staging lane under the integration lock and verifies that exact tree; the owner's `done` is legal only once the journal proves a verified integration, publishes under the generation check and records `accepted` with the transition; a moved parent records `integration_stale` and is prepared again, on this call or the next `done`. `crates/runtime/src/lane/settle.rs` carries `Lane::candidate` and `Lane::settle`, `prepare` and `publish`: a fast-forward when git can, else the ref alone with the checkout following on every path the user did not touch, never over the user's own file in an untracked directory, always from the repository root, and never on a fast-forward failure that is not the user's dirt. `fail`, `drop`, `supersede` and the reap record a disposition before the lane and slot go, a lane that cannot settle stays held on its record, and `delete` refuses a worktree without one; a second `submit` of one token shares its verification as `done` does; the probe loop reports a stuck child once per episode. The forge issue for the stage is opened at land time. Review fix (Refs #426): a run killed between the publication and the `accepted` record left HEAD at the integrated commit, which the next `done` read as a moved parent and prepared again; a parent already at the integrated commit now proceeds to the acceptance record with `how` null (`lanes::accept::a_publication_killed_before_its_record_is_accepted_by_the_next_done`); and the staging session is journaled as `integration_intent` before `prepare` claims its lane, so the next preparation frees the slot and deletes the branch a killed run left and records that on its own intent (`lanes::accept::a_staging_lane_killed_before_its_record_is_dropped_by_the_next_done`). growth +11365: `plan/acceptance.rs`, `lane/settle.rs`, `plan/capacity.rs`, the typed acceptance records in `crates/types/src/plan/acceptance.rs` and the stuck notice, measured against the 0.259.0 `src_loc.json` baseline main still carries, so the four stages' own +10572 and main's unabsorbed +793 ride inside it (D195); weighed for deletion, the B11 `merge_worktree` hand-back, kept: F0e did not measure it, so it stays the road for `rlm.run` worktree children, and it refuses a plan-dispatched child (`take_settled_worktree`), whose worktree lands only through this acceptance, which the engine drives from D225 (#483). |

This is stage F0c of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11, sections 6.1 to 6.5). `crates/types/src/plan/contract.rs` is the contract type: an item list of `cmd`, `schema` and `example` deciders with a class floor, weights, a threshold and a coverage floor, aggregated by section 6.2, and a `judge` item refused at declaration until F3a. `Todo.contract` and `Todo.attempt` land on the document; D77's ladder fields are gone and old records park them in `extra`. `plan/verify.rs` is the verifier: `start` freezes the contract and its criteria by digest, `done` commits `verification_requested { token, effect_id, claim }` under the lease, releases it, runs every item under one whole-verification deadline that also reads the session deadline of D177 and per-item deadlines (`/bin/sh -c` by absolute path, `env_clear()` plus the manifest's declared names, its own process group killed as a tree, capped output, every `protected` path digested before and after the run), re-acquires the lease, compares the whole token and commits either the verdict with `Done { VerifiedDone }` as one record or `done_refused { verdict }`; a moved token commits `verification_stale` and charges nothing; the third refused verdict on one attempt steps the todo `Blocked { on: User }` as a recorded transition. Review fix (Refs #425): a declared output schema with no output resolver attached refuses `done` as an unserved product instead of passing it unread, and `yi plan done` reads the product and the schema through a workspace resolver as the session does (`plan_ops::a_declared_output_with_no_resolver_is_refused`, `yi-cli plan_ops::cli_done_validates_a_declared_output_schema`). growth +3173: D194's contract type, the verifier, the token, the journal kinds `verification_requested`, `done_refused` and `verification_stale`, the done path in `plan/done.rs`, the closure of every completion path, the materialized verification workspace with its shadow-gitdir and tree-hash snapshotters, and the F0c fixtures under `fixtures/plans/contracts/`; D77's unread ladder fields were deleted. |
| 0.266.0 | 2026-09-13 | The plan store is an append-only journal with a typed JSON checkpoint and artifact blobs; recovery reconstructs without effects and reconciles the rest; hand edits are gone and a user's administrative ops are confirmed in the session (D193, amends D97 and D105; carries D26 and D53; Closes #424). The second review round fixed: a reused `request_id` is refused across op kinds, not only across args (`plan_journal::a_reused_request_id_with_another_op_kind_is_refused`); a confirmed op binds the root the human saw; a failed first append removes the empty journal it created, so the store is not wedged (`plan_journal::a_failed_first_append_leaves_no_journal_behind`); an imported graph runs the domain validator before its genesis record (`plan_import::an_invalid_graph_is_refused_before_the_genesis_record`); the artifact directory is synced after the blob rename; `yi plan import <id>` and `yi plan import local://<path>` parse as the store prints them (`plan_ops::the_import_commands_the_store_prints_run_as_printed`); `load` reads the journal once. growth +3939: the journal, its reducer and recovery, explicit import with artifact blobs and the confirmed user path, measured for D193; the Markdown writer and the hand-edit fold were deleted for them, and the plan skill's Python went with F0a. Round 3 of the review: `admit` is the engine's own refusal on `start` (`PlanOpError::Admission` with the count, before any spawn intent; `admissible` is deleted and the held list is derived from the same refusals); the record cap is rehearsed before the first reap with an 8 KiB envelope for the reaped urls, so an over-cap `supersede` kills nothing; effect ids carry the clock like request ids and the reducer refuses a duplicate `spawn_intent`; the session engine's liveness is the host's hold, so `repair` lists only children the host no longer holds; the bare `plan://<id>` address reads through the journal and regenerates a lost checkpoint; the fuzz lane gains `Import`. The narrower design was one always-option with no label, which is what shipped before and is what the incident is: a grant nobody can see the scope of. Deleting the static `OPTIONS` paid part of it back; `CallOutcome::identity` and the `denied` argument went with D206, which is where the memory that read them was replaced. A structured tool's session rule was keyed on its whole arguments JSON, patch body and all, so "Allow always" on an edit never matched the next edit and the user was asked again for every file; a command's rule was keyed on its exact text. The prompt now offers what the grant would cover: `Always allow edits under crates/tui/src`, `Always allow edits anywhere in this tree`, or ``Always allow `git worktree` in this tree``, and keeps the one chosen. A grant is bounded by the tree its label names: a command is grantable only when its program is named on PATH, not by path, and carries no `-C`, `--git-dir` or `--work-tree`, so `git -C /elsewhere worktree remove --force` is not covered by a granted `git worktree`; a write at the tree root offers the exact call first, so `a` never hands over the repository. A later path write is allowed when every target sits under a granted directory inside the working tree; a later command when its single unproven program and verb is granted, and never when a segment is destructive, reaches the network, carries an env-prefixed argv0, or cannot be parsed. Catastrophic targets and configured denies still refuse. Seen red: `decide::a_scope_grant_keeps_the_promise_its_label_makes` (a granted `git worktree` allowed `git -C /home/user/other-repo worktree remove --force`, and a granted `python3` allowed `./python3`), `decide::a_root_level_edit_offers_the_exact_call_before_the_tree` (a README edit offered only the tree, and `a` then allowed every file in the repository), `permission_scope::always_allow_on_an_edit_does_not_ask_for_the_next_edit_in_that_dir` (two asks for two edits in one directory), `tui_drive::an_always_allowed_directory_edits_without_a_second_prompt` (the real binary asked for the second write and the drive never idled), `decide::a_directory_grant_allows_later_edits_beneath_it_only`, `decide::a_scope_grant_allows_the_same_subcommand_with_other_arguments`, `decide::grants_offer_the_target_dir_then_the_tree_root`, `tui_e2e::an_approval_offers_one_always_option_per_grant` (one unnamed `Allow always` row), `acp_unit::permission_bridge_writes_the_request_and_maps_the_selected_outcome` (`allow_always_1` read as a rejection), `port::option_for_picks_the_nth_always_option_and_labels_it`. |
| 0.261.0 | 2026-09-15 | A command the sandbox refused asks the next time, whatever else the retry adds (D206, #441). growth +234: the scope reader and the grant's own bounds, over both stages — the git-dir reader (D205) and this one, each a dozen lines of file reads that replace a git spawn and an exact-text memory; the lenient splitter was weighed against reusing `parse` alone and earns its lines by escalating what the parser refuses to read. On the 2026-09-14 session the sandbox refused `git worktree add`, the hint promised that running the same command again would ask, and the model's retry appended `&& git status --porcelain \| wc -l`; the refusal was remembered by exact text, so the retry was contained again and no question ever surfaced. Now a refusal is remembered by the program and verb it ran (`git worktree`, `cargo fmt`, `mkdir`), any later contained command using one of them asks, and the hint says exactly that: "`mkdir` now needs permission: the next call using it asks instead of running contained, and is refused where nobody can answer". Seen red: `tui_drive::a_reshaped_retry_of_a_refused_program_reaches_the_approval_view` (the real binary contained the reshaped retry and never drew the approval view), `sandbox_seam::a_refused_program_asks_even_when_the_retry_text_differs` (the second `mkdir … && ls` was contained, not asked), `safety::scope_names_program_and_subcommand` (no scopes), `sandbox::a_denial_is_only_claimed_when_the_output_says_so` (the hint named no scope and promised a byte-identical rerun). |
| 0.260.0 | 2026-09-15 | Git runs inside the sandbox (D205, #440). On the 2026-09-14 session that fixed `/new`, the model worked in a lane, a detached worktree whose index and refs live in the trunk's `.git`; the sandbox wrote only the lane and tmp, so `git add` failed on `.git/worktrees/yi-auto-pool/index.lock` and `git worktree add` on its ref lock, and the user ran git by hand. A contained command may now write the worktree's git dir and the common dir — except `hooks`, `config` and the `commondir`/`gitdir` pointers, which host git runs or reads back, and which a contained process must not be able to plant. Deleting either dir is denied in every mode, and the denial now reads through wrappers, so `nice -n 5 rm -rf .git` is refused like the bare spelling. A `gitdir:` pointer is believed only when it names a directory holding a `HEAD`, so repository data cannot make `/` a writable root. `git fetch`, `pull` and `push` ask because a contained run has no network, and `git -C <dir> reset --hard` reads as the destructive `reset` it is. A headless drive takes `--faux <cassette>` so a journey can script the model's tool calls. Seen red: `tui_drive::a_lane_session_commits_contained` (the real binary's contained commit in a lane never reached the object store), `sandbox::a_contained_git_commit_succeeds_in_a_linked_worktree` (`index.lock`: Operation not permitted), `sandbox::a_contained_command_cannot_write_a_hook_or_the_config` (the hook was written, and would have run on the host), `sandbox::a_linked_worktree_sandbox_writes_its_gitdir_and_common_dir`, `decide::git_dirs_follow_a_worktree_pointer_to_its_common_dir`, `decide::git_dirs_refuse_a_pointer_that_is_not_a_git_dir` (`gitdir: /` resolved to `/`), `decide::a_wrapped_destructive_command_is_denied_in_every_mode` (`nice -n 5 rm -rf <common>` only asked), `decide::rm_rf_of_a_linked_worktrees_common_dir_is_denied_in_every_mode`, `safety::git_network_verbs_ask_for_egress`, `safety::git_global_options_do_not_become_the_subcommand`. |

The clamp to 600 s was silent until a run hit it: in the 2026-09-11 v4 sweep telecom-entity-resolution asked `timeout_secs: 900` on four calls, three returned within 66 s with no word of the cap, and the fourth learned the ceiling from `[timed out after 600s; …]` after 605 s; it asked 600 from then on. Every result of a call that asks past 600 now opens with `[timeout_secs 900 capped at 600]`, whether the command returned, timed out or became a job under `auto_background`; the note would not have saved that run, whose quadratic merge outran 600 s anyway. The default rises to 300 s, the wait clamp's top, and the description and the schema name it beside the 600 s ceiling. Replayed over the sweep's 999 bash calls, five calls in five trials hit the 120 s default: in freecad-impeller, vpp-loss-divergence and vllm-deepseek-streaming the retries finished inside 300 s (226.7 s; one of two back-to-back runs in 65.4 s; the five tool runs alone in 125.2 s), so each saves a turn and about 120 s; in freight-dispatch-shift and payments-pipeline-fix a server started with `&` held the capture pipe, so the command hangs to the new default and loses 180 s more. A hung default command in the last turn is now cut by the deadline rather than returned, since 300 s exceeds the 240 s stop margin. Tests: `tools::a_capped_timeout_secs_opens_every_result_of_the_call` (telecom's recorded arguments, returned and backgrounded), `builtins::tests::the_bash_description_names_the_timeout_default_and_ceiling`. growth +231: measured at the push under D142; this version adds 27 src lines (the note 10, the default and its comment 2, the description pin 15), and the other 204 are 0.235.0 to 0.238.0 against the 0.234.0 baseline. |
| 0.238.0 | 2026-09-12 | A timed-out command's descendants that left its process group die with it (amends D142; Refs #100). In the v4 sweep telecom-entity-resolution ran `cd /app && timeout 900 <venv>/bin/python solve.py` and read `[the process outlived the kill and keeps running as job 6; …]`: GNU timeout puts itself and its child in a new group, so the group kill reached the shell and left both holding the capture pipes. `kill_tree` now reads the process table before any signal (`/proc/<pid>/stat` on Linux, split right of comm's closing paren; `/bin/ps -eo pid=,ppid=` on macOS), collects every pid whose parent chain reaches the shell, and after the group and shell kills sends those pids one KILL through the shell builtin; the walk goes first because a re-grouped child reparents to init once its parent dies. A table that cannot be read, or a pid kill that cannot spawn, is named in `[group kill failed: …]` instead of reading as no descendants. One escape remains: a re-grouped descendant whose parent had already exited before the cancel is init's child, so the walk never reaches it and it runs to its own end; a subreaper or a cgroup would close that, and both need libc (§13.1). Tests: `jobs::tests::the_sweeps_timeout_python_chain_dies_at_the_bash_timeout` (the trial's `cd … && timeout … python … && ls` chain through the bash timeout; unfixed, it reads the sweep's `outlived the kill` line) and `process::tests::a_grandchild_that_left_the_group_dies_with_the_tree` (a `preexec_fn=os.setpgrp` grandchild, python 3.2 and later; unfixed, it held stdout for 30 s); both want a `ready` line first, so a missing `python3` fails instead of skipping. growth +204: measured at the push under D142; this version adds about 85 src lines and 57 test lines, and the other 62 are 0.235.0 to 0.237.0 against the 0.234.0 baseline. |

| 0.222.0 | 2026-09-10 | A continued session's kernel revives in the next process (amends D87; Refs #100). The root kernel's directory was its process's `rlm-<pid>`, so `--continue` looked for `<id>.kernel-state.dill` under the new pid, restored nothing, and a cell naming a variable from the replayed transcript raised NameError; the audit archive holds one bucket per process (`rlm-283/`, `rlm-288/`, `rlm-302/`) under one sessions directory. `RuntimeWiring::kernel_dir` is the sessions directory at depth 0, where `snapshot_paths` already keys the file by session id, and a child's own `sub-*` directory otherwise; the kernel's snapshot, `RLM_SESSION_DIR` and sandbox writable root all read it, so the root kernel writes where bash already could. The kernel side's local harness store follows `RLM_SESSION_DIR` to `sessions/harness/`, so it outlives the process and is one store for every session under that directory, in every project; an `rlm.harness` entry written in one project is read in the next. `rlm-<pid>` keeps the family directory, the schedule ledger and the children, so `main.rs` and the `serve_daemon` ledgers are untouched. A root whose store failed to open still boots unkeyed, and its `kernel-state.dill` is now shared with the next such run rather than kept per process. `/new` and RPC `new_session`, `switch_session` and `fork` swap the store under a live kernel, which kept writing under the first session's id; with the file now in the shared directory that would overwrite the first session's snapshot with the next one's names, so `KernelService` reboots a kernel whose snapshot path no longer matches the attached store, as it already did for a changed sandbox, and the new session starts from its own snapshot or none. Tests: `snapshot_e2e::the_snapshot_dir_is_the_sessions_dir_not_the_process_dir`, two root wirings under pids 4711 and 4890 resuming one session id, the second printing the first's `answer` (red: `the root's snapshot sits flat in the sessions dir, not under rlm-<pid>`; it sat in `sessions/rlm-4711/`), `snapshot_e2e::a_store_switch_rekeys_the_live_kernel`, a store switch mid-process whose second session must not see the first's `answer` and whose first session's snapshot must not gain the second's names (red: `the new session's kernel starts from its own namespace: True`). growth +14867: measured at the push under D87; this version adds about twenty src lines. |
| 0.221.0 | 2026-09-10 | A control byte no longer re-forms a fence header in the prompt yard (amends D98; Refs #100). The yard's sanitizer escaped the `<<<` sentinel before it dropped control characters, so an `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md` carrying `<<\0<yi-external … trust="granted">>>` reached the prompt as an unescaped header labeled granted; on Terminal-Bench the working tree is task-authored, so any trial could carry one. Its nonce is wrong, but nothing reads a fence back except the model. `fetch` had fixed the same order in its own copy of the literals. One `pub(crate)` sanitizer in `ext/assemble.rs` now strips control characters first and escapes last, `fence_untrusted` calls it, and fetch's copy is deleted. A run of four or more `<` still leaves one `<<<` after the escape, `<<<<yi-external` becoming `<\<<<yi-external`, with or without a control byte; that predates this version and is left to a follow-up. Tests: `injection_canary::poisoned_project_text_stays_in_the_yard`, whose poison gains the smuggled header and which now asserts its escaped form (red: `every fence closes exactly once; the forged closer is escaped`, `left: 2`, `right: 3`); `fetch::tests::fenced_content_cannot_close_its_own_fence` stays green on the shared function. growth +14849: measured at the push under D98; this version removes about eight src lines. |
| 0.220.0 | 2026-09-10 | A read is refused only at a key store, the workspace `.git` or a device (D180, amends D15; Refs #100). Every target went through the whole catastrophic list before the mode was read, so under `--yolo` a `read` of `/etc/nginx/nginx.conf` or `/usr/include/stdio.h` was denied while `bash cat` printed the same bytes. A call that only reads and reports itself reversible is now judged by `read_is_catastrophic`: the workspace `.git`, a path at or under a credential store in home, a directory above one (grep's walk of `/` or `~` would carry into `~/.ssh`), and `/dev`, where `/dev/zero` never ends and `/dev/tty` waits; every other call meets the whole list as before, and `command_reads_credentials` shares the credential-store list. `/etc`, `/usr`, `/var` and `/proc` become readable, `/proc/self/environ` included, which `bash env` already printed, but not a `/proc` path through a link (`/proc/self/root`, `cwd`, `fd`, `map_files`), which reaches a key or the `.git` by another name; `/`, `/home`, `/Users` and `/root` stay refused whatever HOME is, since other users' key stores sit under them. A tool's default `irreversible` now follows the call's own kind, so grep's replace+apply, which the gate took for a read, keeps the whole list, asks in ask mode, and in auto runs unasked only on a `path` inside the working tree. The yolo fragment names what a read may not touch. Tests: `decide::a_read_of_a_system_path_is_allowed_but_a_key_is_not` (red: `/etc/nginx/nginx.conf in Ask: Deny { reason: "read targets a protected path (/etc/nginx/nginx.conf); this is denied in every mode." }`), `tools::a_grep_that_rewrites_is_flagged_irreversible` (red: `apply writes every hit`). growth +14857: measured at the push under D180; this version adds about 45 src lines. |
| 0.219.0 | 2026-09-10 | A capped command stream keeps its end as well as its start (amends D161; Refs #100). `drain_capped` kept each stream's first 30,000 bytes and dropped the rest, so a `pytest -q` whose failures ran past that lost its `FAILED` lines and its `N failed, M passed` summary from the result, from D161's reduced view and from the file its `[full output: path]` pointer names, and a finished job's report showed the opposite end from its live polls. Each stream now keeps the first half and a rolling last half of its cap, with `[N bytes omitted from the middle]` between them; the counters saturate, each half is decoded on its own (a character split at a seam becomes U+FFFD), and a stream that fits is decoded whole. Every `run_captured` caller shares the change, so a document conversion's closing status line and a failed goal check's last lines also survive a long stream. The raw stream is still not teed: that needs the recovery directory threaded through `run_captured`, and the audit slice's 268 bash calls capped none. Tests: `tools::a_capped_bash_stream_keeps_the_tail_with_the_verdict` (a 43,923-byte stream keeps its first rows, its last line and the exact omitted count), `process::tests::an_uncut_stream_is_decoded_whole`. growth +14812: measured at the push under D161; this version adds about 20 src lines. |
| 0.218.0 | 2026-09-10 | The repeat breaker counts a batch over the last six turns and sees a text-only turn (D179, amends D152; Refs #100). D152 compared a turn only with the one before it and gave a turn with no tool call no signature, so `bash ls` and `todo view` taking turns, a call repeated between job polls and a reply repeated to an intercept that keeps re-driving were never counted: in the audit slice 147 of 1,044 turns repeated a batch, four of them back to back, and the breaker fired once, while cargo Lb45bGG spent 273 of its 444 turns and about $2.05 answering one intercept. Each turn's batch, or the text of a turn with no calls collapsed to single spaces and cut at 256 characters, now joins a window of the last six; a third copy in the window sends the hidden steer once the last three turns each repeated a batch already in the window, and the sixth, which must fill the window, ends the run, so two batches taking turns are steered once and never ended. The steer goes once per stretch, until a batch new to the window shows progress: two batches taking turns hold the count at three, and a steer on every such turn would itself keep two replies re-driving without end. A fresh edit before each run of one check is new to the window, so that loop is never steered, and an identical batch draws the steer at its fourth run rather than its third. A follow-up prompt starts an empty window, as it starts its own cut count. A job poll and a cut turn, which keeps no text, still count for nothing, and the message no longer claims the same result, which was never compared. Replayed over 253 archived sessions the rule sends 11 steers in 4 sessions where D152 sent 6 in 3, none with an edit in the five turns before, and would have ended Lb45bGG at turn 215; a steer at any third copy sent 31 in 15, and four of them landed on an edit and a check taking turns, two of them in v4 trials. Tests: `loop_events::an_alternating_pair_and_a_text_only_spiral_are_broken` (red: two calls taking turns for eight turns drew no steer, `left: 0`; the same reply with its whitespace varied, answering an intercept that always re-drives, draws one steer and ends at the sixth, and four cut turns draw none), `loop_events::a_follow_up_prompt_starts_its_own_repeat_window` (red: the same answer to a prompt and two follow-ups drew a steer, `left: 1`), `loop_events::an_edit_then_check_loop_is_not_steered` (red on a steer at any third copy: five fresh edits, each followed by the same `cargo test`, drew three steers, `left: 3`). growth +14795: measured at the push under D179; this version adds about 25 src lines. |
| 0.217.0 | 2026-09-10 | Reasoning cuts are counted per prompt, and a truncated call is a length strike (D178, amends D153 and D168; Refs #100). Any turn with a tool call zeroed both the cut and the length count, so D168's twelve cuts in a row was never approached: the audit slice's photonic trials were cut 22 times, never more than four in a row, while the cuts took two thirds of each trial's request wall. A tool call now leaves the cut count alone and the prompt's sixth cut ends the run, while a follow-up prompt queued behind it starts its own count; the re-drive that names the write follows the prompt's second cut. A length stop that carries a call still fails it and asks for it whole, and now counts toward the three length strikes instead of resetting them: a call past the 32,768-token output ceiling comes back truncated with new partial arguments, which neither the repeat breaker nor the reset count could end. Only a turn whose calls run resets the length count. One check after the turn covers repeats, length stops and cuts; it runs before the steering queue is read, so a bare length stop now counts even with a steer waiting, which it did not before. Tests: `loop_events::cut_turns_are_counted_per_prompt_not_per_tool_call` (red: six cuts between tool calls re-drove at rungs `[1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1]` and ran on to the answer), `loop_events::a_length_stop_carrying_a_truncated_call_is_a_strike` (red: three truncated calls ran on to a fourth turn), `loop_events::a_follow_up_prompt_starts_its_own_cut_count` (red: a follow-up's first cut, after five in the prompt before it, ended the run). growth +14768: measured at the push under D178; this version adds two src lines. |
| 0.216.0 | 2026-09-10 | `--deadline` is enforced, not only shown (D177, amends D150; Refs #100). D150 put the clock in the environment block and nothing read it, so a command or a kernel cell that outlived the budget held its turn until harbor killed the container. The session now holds one `Deadline`, set once from the wiring, and three readers share it: the environment line; the tool cancel flag, which fires at zero the way Esc does, so bash's watchdog kills the process group and the kernel watcher interrupts the cell; and the loop's `should_stop_after_turn`, which ends the run after the turn in flight once 240 s, or a quarter of a shorter budget, is all that is left. The turn is never cut: it settles and `yi ask` exits 0. The margin is 240 s, not 120 s, because the longest request measured, a settled reasoning cut, ran about 170 s and one that starts inside the margin must finish before harbor's kill; a flat margin would be longer than the whole 120 s and 180 s budgets of six fixtures and end each of their runs after one turn. A child session starts its own clock from the same total, and a headless TUI drive's `--deadline` now bounds its session as well as its script. `yi ask` also read a lagged event stream as the end of the run and exited 0 mid-turn; it now prints the count to stderr and reads on. `run_serve_command` moves to shells.rs beside the other launchers, since main.rs sat at its 1,200-line ceiling. Tests: `session_faux::a_deadline_kills_a_running_bash_call` (red: a 1 s deadline left `sleep 30` running past 5 s), `session_faux::a_deadline_ends_the_run_after_the_turn_in_flight` (red: the second reply was requested after a 4.6 s call ran past a 6 s budget's margin), `session::deadline::tests::the_stop_margin_is_four_minutes_or_a_quarter_of_the_budget`. growth +14766: measured at the push under D177; this version adds about 90 src lines. |
| 0.215.0 | 2026-09-10 | A kernel cell has a wall clock, and an abandoned snapshot no longer refuses the next cell (D176; Refs #100). The ipython watcher's only exit was the interrupt, so a `while True:` loop, a blocking read or a `%%bash` cell held the turn until harbor killed the container. A cell is now interrupted at `cell_ceiling` on the kernel service's options, bash's 600 s ceiling unless set, on a clock that starts after the kernel boots, and the tool's description says so. The debounced snapshot's five-second abort forced its cell to `Aborted` after the grace but kept it in the active slot, so the user's next cell waited out the busy window and ended `BusyAfterInterrupt`, which restarts the kernel with at most its last finished snapshot; Yi's own cells now give the slot up, and a user cell keeps it for the busy-reuse recovery. Clearing the slot alone lost the next cell instead: ipykernel aborts every request queued behind a failed one, and Yi read the aborted request as an empty success, so internal cells go out with `stop_on_error` off. A `%%bash` cell comes back aborted at the ceiling, but its shell runs on: IPython's magic ends the cell on a `TypeError` without stopping it (a probe's child wrote its file after the abort), and 0.214.0's group kill covers the bash tool, not the kernel. A child handle's `result` waited 900 s by default and the doctrine and orchestrate examples waited 900 s in one cell, which the ceiling now cuts at 600 s before the child's answer or the documented `TimeoutError` arrives; the default is 540 s and the examples, which wait 120 s first in the same cell, collect with `timeout=420`. Tests: `prompts::no_prompt_example_waits_past_the_cell_ceiling` (red on the 900 s examples: `an example waits 1020 s in one cell`), `ipython_faux::a_cell_that_never_returns_is_aborted_at_the_ceiling` (a 1 s ceiling returns the output so far and `[cell aborted]`), `kernel_e2e::an_aborted_internal_cell_clears_the_active_slot` (an internal cell deaf to the interrupt for 10 s, then failing on it; the next cell returns `'alive'`). growth +14676: measured at the push under D176; this version adds about 20 src lines. |
| 0.214.0 | 2026-09-10 | A timed-out command's process group is killed without a `kill` binary (amends D142; Refs #100). `kill_tree` spawned `kill -KILL -- -<pgid>` through PATH and dropped the result; slim benchmark images ship no `kill(1)` (Debian's lives in `procps`), so the ENOENT went unseen and a timed-out `python3` outlived its shell and kept running as a job on three trials. The group kill now runs `/bin/sh -c 'kill -KILL "-$1"'`, the shell's builtin, in the one form dash and bash both parse (dash refuses `--` and `-s KILL`), and goes before the shell's own kill, since macOS answers EPERM for a group whose only member is a zombie, and not at all once the shell is reaped, when its pid may name another group; a kill that fails is named as `[group kill failed: …]` in the bash result, or in the job's late report when the surviving group held the pipes past the kill's five-second grace. The kernel's bootstrap lock and the plan store's lease probe their holder the same way, and a probe that cannot run no longer reads as a dead holder whose lock may be taken: `process_is_running` returns the error and `lock_is_stale` leaves the call to the lock's age, as for a lock without a pid. The lane warmer's stop probes and signals through the builtin too, and fails on a probe that cannot run instead of resetting the slot under a live install. | 0.207.0 | 2026-09-10 | `read` converts office documents and PDFs through the kernel venv (D171; Closes #368). `firecrawl-anydoc` and `pdf-inspector` join the default extras, the venv directory's name hashes the extras beside the ready check so this venv and main's never rebuild each other, and a `YI_KERNEL_PYTHON` may lack the two wheels. A file whose head carries a zip, OLE2, `%PDF-` or `{\rtf` marker goes to the venv's python once, under a 256 MiB ceiling (1 GiB with `pages=`), a 30 s timeout and, on macOS, seatbelt with no network and the cache dir as its one writable root (a user and network namespace on Linux where `unshare` allows one); the converter sniffs the content, so a `.docx` named `.txt` converts. csv is read as text; Latin-1 and UTF-16 text is decoded with a note; an image is named as one; a NUL-bearing file that is no document is refused as binary. The Markdown goes to `~/.yi/converted/<stem>-<key>.<hash>.<kind>.md` (dir mode 0700, copy read-only), keyed by the source's path, length and byte hash and by the Markdown's own hash: a saved original misses, a forged copy is converted again, parallel first reads each stage under their own name, and copies older than 30 days or past 256 MiB in total are evicted. The copy is read through the ordinary window under the original's path (no path outside the working tree reaches the model), unclipped, with a heading outline when capped and a heading section for `find=`; `edit` and `write` refuse any existing binary document or NUL-bearing file and any path under the copy dir, while RTF and decoded text stay writable. PDF pages arrive under `[page N]` markers, `pages="3-5"` reads only those, and pages without a text layer are named and left out; only a PDF with no text page is refused. Spreadsheets lose empty rows and cell padding and gain a `[sheets: …]` line. A glob charges the Markdown, not the bytes on disk. OCR is pinned to `reject` and nothing reaches the hosted mode. The bootstrap records the wheel's formats and aliases as `documentFormats` beside a hash of the probe, refreshed at the next readiness check when the probe changes; `read`'s description lists exactly those, asked again on every request, or nothing when the venv recorded none; `bash` names `read` when a command reaches for `pdftotext`, `pandoc` or `textutil`. Round three, from playing Yi on two terminal-bench tasks and a coursework folder: a PDF page with a detected table or two columns arrives as plain text in reading order under a note that the conversion is lossy (a row stays whole; columns read left then right; headings restored), running headers are left out once, decks carry `[slide N]` markers, a one-page PDF carries no page marker, sheets lose empty columns and point a sheet past 500 rows at `pandas.read_excel` (`openpyxl` joins the extras), `grep` searches documents through their Markdown and names what it could not search, a glob converts only while it has room, a copy's first look is 12 KB with its outline first (shallowest levels, sampled, repeated titles once), `find=` folds typographic quotes, a copy adds no code refs, the kernel mutes pip's version notice, and the ipython description names `anydoc`, `pdf_inspector` and pandas. The math task's answer keys now parse 48/48 from `read`'s text alone (47 before), and the schedule question that came back with a neighbour's slot now comes back right. Two dogfood runs over 141 real documents from this machine: 118 converted (cold median about 110 ms, 5.3 s at most on a 239 MB Latin-1 csv), 17 refused with a stated reason (8 PDFs with no text page, 1 past the ceiling, 6 of anydoc's resource or malformed refusals, 1 encrypted, 1 Word lock file), 2 decoded, 4 read as the text they were. The plan's phase gate (docs/plans/2026-09-09-anydoc-and-pdf-inspector.md §9) was waived by the owner, so all three phases ship here; §12 there records the departures. Tests: `documents::{a_docx_reads_as_markdown_through_the_read_window, the_copy_is_reused_until_the_original_changes, write_and_edit_refuse_a_converted_original, a_scanned_pdf_is_refused_with_its_reason, the_content_names_the_format_not_the_extension, a_binary_that_is_no_document_keeps_the_plain_error, an_rtf_file_converts_though_it_is_seven_bit_text, a_partly_scanned_pdf_reads_its_text_pages_and_names_the_rest, parallel_first_reads_of_one_document_all_succeed, a_file_past_the_ceiling_is_refused_before_the_converter_runs, a_hanging_converter_is_stopped_at_the_timeout_and_on_cancel, a_document_with_a_unicode_name_converts, a_document_whose_name_is_not_utf8_converts, a_tampered_copy_is_converted_again, write_refuses_an_unread_document_and_allows_text_formats, text_that_is_not_utf8_is_decoded_rather_than_refused, a_glob_reads_documents_whole_and_counts_their_markdown, markdown_gets_an_outline_when_capped_and_find_returns_its_section, a_spreadsheet_loses_its_empty_rows_columns_and_padding, a_large_sheet_points_at_a_pandas_route_that_works, a_pdf_table_and_columns_arrive_as_reading_order_text_under_a_note, running_headers_are_left_out_once_and_body_lines_kept, a_one_page_pdf_carries_no_page_marker, a_deck_reads_slide_by_slide_with_its_notes, a_long_document_opens_with_its_outline_and_a_short_first_look, find_matches_typographic_quotes_and_adds_no_code_refs, grep_searches_documents_through_their_markdown_and_never_rewrites_them, a_glob_lists_what_it_does_not_convert_and_why}`, `prompt_drift::read_names_exactly_the_formats_the_installed_wheel_converts`, yi-tools `documents::{the_description_claims_only_the_recorded_formats, an_unbuilt_venv_is_named_beside_the_plain_error, the_description_follows_a_venv_built_mid_session, bash_points_a_habitual_converter_at_read}`, `bootstrap::tests::{the_default_venv_is_keyed_by_the_ready_check, the_converter_reads_back_the_formats_its_venv_recorded, a_kernel_python_may_lack_the_converter_wheels}`. growth +14511: measured at the push under D171; this version adds about 1520 src lines, the converter, its refusals, the copy cache and the Markdown resolver in one yi-tools module and one resolver, since a `convert` tool (a prompt cost on every request) and the in-process crate (3.42 MiB) were both weighed and refused. |
| 0.206.0 | 2026-09-09 | A stream that dies mid-turn is settled from the generation record (amends D79 and D163; Closes #331). An upstream drop after the first chunk (a 502 from a slow provider, D146's retry case) ended the turn as an error with `unknown` usage, and under D79 that voided the trial's cost: the 07:10 foodstuff job carried one and the slice driver refused cargo on it, the fifth stop of the night. The completions path now settles such a turn from the record the way 0.201.0 settles a cut, behind one `settle_from_record`, and emits its error with the tokens it cost; a stream that died before any chunk has no record and stays unknown. The retry rule is unchanged (`nothing_delivered` reads the same usage). Tests: `openai_mapper::a_stream_that_dies_mid_turn_keeps_the_usage_it_reported`. growth +12995: measured at the push under D168; this version adds about 15 src lines. |
| 0.205.0 | 2026-09-09 | A cut stream ends as a length `Done` (amends D163; Closes #327). After the pump returned at the cut flag, `ChunkMapper::finish` saw no finish reason and emitted `Stream ended without finish_reason` as an error; the loop's drain returned on it, the cut message was built from the pre-stream partial, and the usage 0.201.0 had settled from the generation record was never emitted. 0.202.0 and 0.204.0 widened the window and the drain around a `Done` that did not exist; the 02:56 run on 0.204.0 still read `unknown: true` on its cut turn. A cut is a length stop by definition: `ChunkMapper::cut` marks the finish and the stop reason before the settle and the finish, so the `Done` carries the record's usage and the id. Tests: `openai_mapper::a_cut_stream_finishes_as_a_length_done_with_its_id`; the loop pins hold. growth +12973: measured at the push under D168; this version adds about 10 src lines. |
| 0.204.0 | 2026-09-09 | The cut drain is bounded by its deadline alone (amends D163; Closes #325). 0.201.0 read at most 256 events after the cut flag; the provider channel holds 256 and a fast upstream has that many thinking deltas queued ahead of the `Done` at the moment of the cut, so the drain gave up on the cap, the cut message was built from the pre-stream partial with no `responseId`, and the settled usage the provider had fetched was thrown away: every photonic cut turn of the 01:48 job read `unknown: true` and the driver stopped a fourth time. The 45-second deadline is now the only bound; the deltas after the cut are bounded by the channel and by the pump, which stops reading at the flag. The loop's faux stream now hands its thinking over in 32-char deltas, so `loop_events::a_cut_turn_keeps_the_usage_the_provider_settled` finds the `Done` behind more than 800 of them. growth +12961: measured at the push under D168; this version removes about 5 src lines. |
| 0.203.0 | 2026-09-09 | A reasoning cut is not a length strike (D168, amends D153 and D162; Closes #323). Both finished photonic attempts of the 01:30 slice run reasoned past 48,000 chars three times in a row after one or two tool turns and D153's third strike ended them with no file written; the artifact steer never fired, being keyed to the third tool turn. Cuts now have their own count of twelve (`CUT_STOP_AT`), the re-drive from the second consecutive cut names the write (`CUT_REDRIVE_TEXT`), and the coupling counts a cut turn beside a tool turn toward the steer. Ordinary length stops keep the rule of three. Tests: `loop_events::consecutive_cuts_re_drive_past_the_third_and_name_the_write`, `a_bare_length_stop_is_re_driven_twice_then_ends_on_the_third` (unchanged), `todo_coupling::cut_turns_count_toward_the_artifact_steer`. growth +12961: measured at the push under D167; this version adds about 25 src lines. |
| 0.202.0 | 2026-09-09 | The cut settlement waits for the generation record (amends D163; Closes #321). 0.201.0 tried the record at 0.5, 2, 5 and 10 s after the drop and the loop drained 15 s for the provider's `Done`; measured at 01:20, the record is a 404 at 2, 5 and 8 s and present at 10 s, so the last try landed on the edge and the drain expired with it: the 01:07 slice run recorded its cut turns with no `responseId` and `unknown: true` again and the driver stopped after html-js-filter a third time. The tries are now at 3, 8, 16 and 28 s and the drain 45 s, paid only on a cut (three turns in nine html-js-filter trials tonight); a trial whose spend cannot be measured is paid in full and counts for nothing. Tests: the 0.201.0 pins hold unchanged. growth +12935: measured at the push under D163; this version adds no src lines. |
| 0.201.0 | 2026-09-09 | A turn cut at the reasoning budget is settled from the generation record (amends D163; Closes #319). The cut drops the stream before its usage chunk, so the turn was recorded `unknown` with a chars/4 estimate, and under D79 an unknown turn voids the trial's cost: the 2026-09-08 slice run stopped after its first task on `spend cannot be measured`. Measured on OpenRouter: a stream dropped at 12,010 chars is billed for what was generated (3,089 completion tokens, $0.00077) and `/generation?id=` serves the record within 20 s. The completions path now fetches that record after a cut (four tries over ten seconds) and its `Done` carries the measured tokens and cost; the loop drains to that `Done` after setting the cut flag (15 s, 256 events at most) and `cut_message` keeps a settled usage, leaving the estimate marked `unknown` only when no record comes. Other providers keep the estimate. Tests: `loop_events::a_cut_turn_keeps_the_usage_the_provider_settled`, `a_reasoning_spiral_is_cut_at_the_char_budget_and_re_driven` (the estimate stays unknown), `settle::tests::a_generation_record_becomes_a_measured_usage`. growth +12934: measured at the push under D167; this version adds about 60 src lines. |
| 0.200.0 | 2026-09-08 | The forge is driven with `fgj` instead of `tea` (D167, amends D122; Closes #312). tea's actions verbs asked for routes this Forgejo does not serve and its open list needed a tsv field spec to parse; fgj is Forgejo-native and `gh`-shaped. The lane adapter opens with `fgj pr create`, reads the open list as `--json` rows by `head.ref`, polls through `fgj api --hostname <origin host>` (`host_of`) and merges with `--merge-method=merge`; `scripts/forge_pr.py` sends bodies on stdin and reads a 404 from stderr; the tracking script, the metadata gate, `docs/FORGE.md` and the two forge skills print fgj fix lines. Tests: `lanes::an_open_pull_request_is_found_by_head` (fgj rows by head ref), `lanes::the_origin_host_is_read_from_ssh_and_https_urls`; the three scripts' selfchecks. growth +12794: measured at the push under D166; this version adds about 10 src lines. |
